## Brianna Burdun

Brianna Burdun  was a priestess of the giant goddess Hiatea and the queen of Hartsvale in 1366 DR.

Description
Because of the giant-blood running in her veins, Brianna was nearly seven feet tall, with a body as rugged as most human males without sacrificing her femininity. Her face was striking, with clear skin, and she had long blonde hair.

Personality
Brianna was a strong-minded and confident individual to the point of stubbornness about what she believed was right; however, if someone showed an incontrovertible proof of her bad judgment, Brianna quickly admitted her mistake and apologized. She otherwise was easy to get along with.

Brianna was intelligent and was very responsible about the rule of her kingdom.

As a follower of Hiatea, she was very serious about matters of childbirth and motherhood. She spent her time in that role counseling the people of her kingdom in matters of family affairs.

Abilities
Brianna could fight with battleaxe, longsword, or spear. She could hold her own as a fighter, although, as queen, she usually had an escort and supported them in any combat with her clerical spells.

Possessions
As queen, she had access to any equipment she needed. She usually carried a few vials of holy water with her and wore her holy symbol, a metal carving of a flaming spear.

Relationships
Brianna was the only child of King Camden of Hartsvale.

As a princess, Brianna fell in love with a firbolg named Tavis Burdun, the owner of the Weary Giant. The two ended up adventuring together, and afterwards were married.

Brianna had a son, Kaedlaw, which she bore after being raped by the father of all ettins, Arno and Julian, but the boy was actually miraculously fathered by Tavis as well.

History

Brianna was born into Hartsvale's royal family. Her father, Camden, in order to win a succession war against his twin brother, allied with an ogre tribe and promised the hand of the yet unborn Brianna to the ogre chieftain. Brianna and most of the people of the vale knew nothing about this agreement. In 1366 DR, the ogre Goboka, who was a follower of the Twilight Spirit, arrived to collect his prize and, together with the king, organized a kidnapping to cover the king's involvement. Brianna's father actively stopped all rescue attempts. Despite this, Tavis Burdun and his friends followed the princess and saved her.

Back in Hartsvale, Brianna, having discovered her father's actions, deposed him and became queen. Some time after this, the ettin Arno and Julain arrived, disguised as a human prince named Arlien, magically seduced and raped Brianna under orders of the Twilight Spirit. Arlien was discovered and defeated but not before impregnating her. Brianna escaped from the palace with only Tavis and the orphan Avner, pursued by many factions that wanted her unborn child. In the end, just after the childbirth, Tavis defeated the Twilight Spirit for good, freeing Brianna and her newborn son.

## Bridget Cymrych

Bridget Cymrych was a Ffolk human princess of Callidyrr on the island of Alaron in the Moonshae Isles in the 7th century DR.

History
In the —during the reign of High King Embro II of Callidyrr, who was known for seeking peace with the Northmen—Princess Bridget of Callidyrr was to marry the Northlander prince Scothgar of Rogarsheim, but she and her whole royal entourage vanished on the voyage to Norland. Ffolk and Northmen each blamed the other for the disappearance, which worsened relations between them.

That same year, Moonshaes Ffolk arrived on the shores of the Utter East, where they chose to settle.Although there is no explicit connection between these events, the implication is clear that Bridget's entourage was somehow transported from the Moonshaes to the shores of the Utter East. In fact, author Brian R. James borrowed this from an article entitled "Perilous Portals: The Sea Gates" written by Mark "Markustay" Taylor in Candlekeep Compendium Volume IX, a fan-written netbook, making it an example of fanon turned canon. Taylor's story tells of a pirate who tried to attack Bridget's treasure-laden ships, until a Northlander fleet intervened. Both Ffolk and Northlanders pursued the pirate into a sea portal, before all three were wrecked in a storm on the Great Sea.

## Brenvol Whitebrow

Brenvol Whitebrow was a werewolf ranger of Selûne and a member of the Fangshields who served as their liaison to the lycanthropes of Faerûn. He believed that his family, the White Pack served as an example to the evil shapechangers of Toril, proof that faith in the Lady of Silver could keep anyone on the path of good and righteousness.

Description
Brenvol was a skilled hunter, tracker and fairly good cook who always preferred to have company while enjoying a campsite meal.

Appearance
The young werewolf had a pointed nose, red cheeks and graying hair (in his human form), that was fully white in some spots, a trait common among his pack members. He had a long and lean frame, similar to that of a wolf.

Personality
He was a friendly and outgoing man who was popular among the Fangshields, and had many among the group who would readily rush to his aid. His good nature made him the perfect contact person for new recruits within the Fangshields.

Relationships
Brenvol's mother was Brena Whitebrow, the alpha female of the White Pack Selûneite lycanthropes. His mother imparted upon him the seriousness and responsibilities of being a leader, preparing him to take over the pack after her.

Possessions
Among Brenvol's possessions were a +1 suit of studded leather armor, a quality heavy mace, shortbow and a elemental gem. He often kept his quiver full of silver arrows and decorated his clothes with custom silver coins imprinted with the holy symbol of Selûne.

As a symbol of the Sultan's rule, the great fountain was huge. It spewed purple flames high into the air from where it sat.

The fountain's fire would die out or dim in the event of the Sultan's death or deposition. The new Sultan would then relight the fires of the fountain in a thunderous explosion easily heard by every occupant of the City of Brass.

## Bracers of the blinding strike

Bracers of the blinding strike were a pair of magical bracers worn on the wrists.

Description
Bracers of the blinding strike looked either like a pair of wrist guards or arm guards.

The bracers weighed 1 lb (0.45 kg).

Powers
In addition to serving as bracers of armor +6, the bracers gave the wearer excellent reaction time in battle and allowed them to attack more swiftly and more often. Originally, the latter two abilities were only usable by people with warrior training, with the acceleration power usable only three times per day. By 1372 DR, the bracers were improved to confer the abilities to anybody and the acceleration was permanent.

History
Bracers of the blinding strike were magic items associated with the drow.

One of the most notable mentions of the bracers of the blinding strike came from the story of a young student of the Monastery of the Dark Moon named Beyl Van Yoren. Her faith in Shar was shaken and she abandoned her studies with the influence of a transmuter named Vergol. As a punishment for her desertion, the elders of the order tasked a group of their best students with tracking her down. They were outfitted with bracers of the blinding strike to counter Beyl's masterful use of the haste spell. The elders did not realize, however, that Beyl was proficient in slow and other hindering spells. The students outfitted with these bracers were never seen ever since.

Notable Owners
Dantrag Baenre: In 1357 DR, the drow weapon master Dantrag Baenre of Menzoberranzan wore bracers of the blinding strike. However, owing to the speed they imparted, Dantrag was unable to improvise during combat. The drow ranger Drizzt Do'Urden was able to exploit Dantrag's weakness, forcing him to stick to rote and practiced combinations, which allowed Drizzt to gain the upper hand.
Drizzt Do'Urden: In 1358 DR, Drizzt wore the bracers over his wrists to increase his attack speed, but due to the sudden lunging of his lightning-quick attacks, he soon realized they restricted and hampered his ability to adapt and improvise during any particular strike or defensive block, whereas normally he could change the direction, speed and angle mid-movement. Since he could not maneuver during any particular strike or defensive block, he moved the bracers to his legs to balance out his agile reflexes.
Gort Ta'alen, the ambitious drow secondboy of house Ta'alen wore a pair of bracers of the blinding strike.
Wesic: a knight from the Order of the Radiant Heart whose saying about striking fast and winning the battle became a popular aphorism. He was leading by example while using the bracers of the blinding strike. Wesic activated the bracers's magic and rushed into battle, often ending it within seconds.
Dayne Lynneth: a merchant from Neverwinter's Blacklake District had a pair of the bracers of the blinding strike in her inventory circa 1374 DR.

## Brian Fargo

Brian Fargo is a computer game producer and designer who founded both Interplay Entertainment and inXile Entertainment.

Career
Fargo began work in the video game industry in 1982. Only a year later, he founded Interplay Entertainment. He was the executive producer of the Forgotten Realms-based Interplay game Blood & Magic, which was released in 1996.

He remained with the company until his resignation in 2000. Two years later, he founded inXile Entertainment with many former Interplay employees.

## Broken Pike

The Broken Pike was a tavern in the subterranean city of Skullport in Undermountain. It was owned and run by Marks in 1370 DR.

Description
A one-story tavern of shabby quality yet average prices, the Broken Pike's patron were a motley crew of fences, pirates, and smugglers. They were a tight-knit bunch who saw all strangers as targets and prey. One had to stay alert and ready for tricks or violence.

Some regulars used the Broken Pike as a venue to plan their crimes, from theft to murder to worse deeds. It was also a meeting place for the Unseen, so some regular patrons were doppelgangers. The duergar fence Thurn Blackskull could be found here many afternoons.

## Bronwyn Daggerford

Bronwyn Daggerford was Duke Pwyll Greatshout's sister and a court wizard in Daggerford.

History
Bronwyn was the sister of Pwyll Greatshout. When she learned that Pwyll had been designated as the heir of Daggerford's duchy by their father Pryden Daggerford, Bronwyn decided to learn the magical arts under the court magician Gwydion pen Dafwyd. Around 1362 DR, Bronwyn went to adventure for almost two years with the adventuring party the Iron Edge.

When she came back, she was considerably more experienced as a wizard and more wealthy.

In 1370 DR, Bronwyn managed the magical duties of the court and trained militiamen in magic.

In 1372 DR both Bronwyn and her brother were missing or presumed dead, while a wealthy merchant and a member of the city council Matagar Bugo proclaimed himself the new Duke. Astriel Shalyn, an agent of the Lords of Waterdeep, discovered Zhentarim's plot to instate a puppet ruler, and take over Daggerford, and for that to succeed the duke and his sister needed to disappear. Matagar Bugo claimed Bronwyn was taken hostage by Xanathar's Thieves' Guild. In reality, the siblings were kept alive in the mines of Illefarn, guarded by Zhent-controlled Griffons Mercenary Company. Astriel's activities led to the Duke and Bronwyn's rescue. The city stood against the united forces of Zhentarim and Gorgomorz, the Devil of Daggerford, who had a personal vendetta with the Daggerford's ducal family. The city stood victorious and Pwyll Greatshout and Bronwyn Daggerford were back on the throne.

## Brown mold

Brown mold was a variety of fungus that grew in response to warmth. So extremely did it absorb heat that it produced an area of cold around it.

Description
Brown mold often grew in subterranean or dungeon environments in large patches of about  in diameter. A patch of this size absorbed enough heat to cool the temperature of the air within a  radius. Within  of the patch of mold, it was cold enough to cause non-lethal injury to some creatures.

Brown mold grew extremely quickly when exposed to great sources of heat such as a nearby flame, doubling in size in only a matter of seconds. In contrast, the mold could be annihilated almost immediately by the effects of a spell dealing cold damage They were also known to be vulnerable to high concentrations of sunlight or ultra-violet light.

Usage
Besides its natural existence as a dungeon hazard, some found practical uses for brown mold, such as using it to cool food pantries, as was done in the Cathedral of Emerald Scales in Hlondeth. In the House of the Moon in Waterdeep, in the west pantry, a small brown mold colony was carefully maintained in a sealed barrel in a small  pit, kept safely under a locked grate to keep people more than  way, and in the refrigerated pantry, a large brown mold colony was contained within a stone tub. Sign on the doors forbade anyone taking fire or any other heat source inside.

Brown mold was also created by the spell mold touch, which was a unique spell available to followers of nature deities Eldath, Mielikki, and Silvanus.

Rumors
The mind flayers were rumored to have developed varieties of brown mold that continued to cool the vicinity around them without harming other life. These varieties were said to grow on illithid homeworlds.

## Brand

Brand was a sadistic wizard spell created by a shig'harak of the Brotherhood of the True Flame in Zakhara.

Effects
Brand was similar to the wizard mark spell save that it could be placed on any surface, including flesh. When cast, this spell burned between 1-6 figures into the subject matter. Living tissue suffered excruciating pain from the experience. Once placed upon an inanimate surface, only the caster of the spell could remove the brand. If placed on living tissue, the caster or a heal spell could remove a brand.

Persons with a brand on a visible part of their body were often shunned by others.

Components
This spell only required verbal and somatic components to cast.

## Broryn Windstag

Broryn Windstag was a noble in Cormyr and a heir of the Windstag noble house during the late 15 century DR.

Personality
Lord Windstag was blustering and bold. He was also a hothead.

Description
Windstag was big and florid.

Abilities
He was good with a blade, and even better at hunting.

Relationships
Lord Traevyn Illance had been a friend and creditor of Broryn Windstag's father; he was the one person Lord Windstag had trusted from youth. He was finally recruited into Illance's faction, which sought to have a Royal Magician from elsewhere mind-controlling the king for the benefit of the nobles.

Lord Windstag had a constant crony, the young Delasko Sornstern. Arclath Delcastle knew him to be associated with Marlin Stormserpent, Mellast Ormblade, Sacrast Handragon and Irlin Stonestable, as well as two other lords around Arclath's own age, Delasko Sornstern and Kathkote Dawntard.

Broryn Windstag was a heir of House Windstag.

History
Broryn Windstag was a guest at the Open Feast of Mirtul 26 in the . He was present for the conversation between Kathkote Dawntard and Orstramagrus, which went well, to his disappointment.

Sornstern was with Broryn Windstag and Kathkote Dawntard when the three of them entered, drunk, to the Dragonriders Club, started a brawl, and attacked Delnor a palace messenger there, almost fighting Arclath Delcastle. They were later repelled by the arrival of the city watch of Suzail, and interrogated by the War Wizards.

After the meeting, they fled towards Marlin Stormserpent's residence at Stormserpent Towers, who involved them at that time in his intrigues involving blueflame items. When Marlin Stormserpent summoned his co-conspirators to tell them about an axe being a third such item, Windstag arrived with great haste together with Sacrast Handragon.

Later, the young lords Dawntard, Sornstern and Windstag carried out a frantic search for the axe; so energetic and haphazard their search was, that they themselves expressly did not understand the intensity of their own actions. Among other actions during their search, they'd offered large amounts of coin in a number of taverns frequented by the nobility, including the Rose and Dragon, the Servant Exalted, and the Hrelto. They were unknowingly controlled by the vampire archmage Manshoon, who had them target Lord Barandror Murandrake's residence, where they finally found the axe.  Though they were pursued by the War Wizard Glathra Barcantle and Swordcaptain Dralkin along the way, and nearly ran out of breath, a spell whisked them away, teleporting them nearly all the way to Stormserpent Towers. After delivering the axe to Marlin Stormserpent, his companions fainted; Windstag delivered the axe with little explanation.

However, the axe brought forth Mirt, one of the Lords of Waterdeep of the previous century, rather than one of the ghosts they sought after. Marlin and Windstag fought him, having decided they could at least seize as many of his properties as possible, even if he could not be commanded. He defeated them soundly, and injured Windstag greatly.

Broryn Windstag was not at the Council of the Dragon; he heard of the calamitous consequences at Court later, at his home in Staghaven House, where he was hosting Irlin Stonestable. Mellast Ormblade and Sacrast Handragon arrived later, confirming what his servant, Aereld, first told him. While mooted there, they were subsequently joined by Traevyn Illance, Osbur, and Marlin Stormserpent; Traevyn recruited them into his faction. He was present when Traevyn Illance plotted a ruse for Marlin to get close to the king by pretending to save him from the blueflame ghosts: Lord Illance called on the Lords present to help him plot. After the moot, Lord Delasko Sornstern had a brief talk with Broryn Windstag about his father Haedro Sornstern's learnings about blueflame items and the Imprisoners, back at the Sornstern Family Gardens. Unknown to them, they were being spied on by the highknight Sir Talonar Winter.

Broryn Windstag was found dead later at Staghaven House alongside all its inhabitants, his body cursed so that anyone who tried to pry information out of it was struck down with insanity.

## Breath of Petron

The Breath of Petron was a powerful dragonborn artifact.

Description
The Breath of Petron was a flute that looked like a root made of red stone, branching in at least six different directions into finer tubes.

Abilities
The Breath of Petron granted those who played it the power to control elemental air, earth, and lightning. The artifact demanded a price for its use, however, turning the lungs of those who played it into crystal.

History
The Breath of Petron was a piece of the lungs of a primordial named Petron. This artifact was made by a slave dragonborn named Haizverad, who stole a small part of the remains of Petron collected by the dragon Versveshardinazar. The Breath of Petron was inherited by the descendants of Haizverad, and used in the Battle of the Crippled Mountain against the red dragon Rhodrolytharnestrix.

After the Spellplague, the dragonborn survivors of Tymanchebar used the Breath of Petron to construct Djerad Thymar, by lifting and fusing the remains of the capital djerad of Tymanchebar, the ruins of the God-Tomb of Nanna-Sin, and rocks from the Smoking Mountains. Since then, the artifact was guarded by members of Clan Shestandeliath.

The Breath of Petron was used again, in Hammer 9 of 1487 DR, by Shestandeliath Geshthax in order to create an earth barrier to protect Djerad Thymar against the advancing Untherite led by Gilgeam.

## Bramas Thorntree

Bramas Thorntree was a thief and smuggler in Tethyr in 1370 DR.

Activities
Bramas worked for Kiros Tamadan, a spice merchant in Athkatla, who through various agents and blind contacts worked for Inselm Hhune.

History
Bramas had  worked for his boss for a long time.

In 1370 DR DR, Bramas was ordered to to gather a mercenary company and recover the Sondarr Scepter from a long-forgotten dwarven tomb in the Starspire Mountains. He gathered some old allies and mercenaries, forming the Company of the Anvil. Together they recovered the Scepter.

They were ordered to maintain a low profile in their return voyage and on Uktar 10 they stopped at the Blue Badger Inn in the village of Toralth for a drink.

Later that night, three dwarves, Bapar Ghalmrin, Odak Truesteel, and Soram Battlebellow, entered the tavern seeking the Sondarr Scepter. However, the dwarves accidentally started a brawl with the Shields of the Highland adventuring company. Bramas and his companions used this as an opportunity to escape with the Scepter.

Personality
Bramas took his work very seriously. He was totally loyal to his masters and always honored a contract. However, he would not follow this duty to the death. Instead, if surrounded by enemies, he falsely surrendered and cooperated with the hope of escaping later. Bramas also enjoyed taunting his vanquished adversaries after the end of a job.

Brindol the Burned was a firesoul genasi and the leader of the genasi faction that lived in the Dominion of the Burned in Glaur as of 1479 DR.

Brindol was a fiery genasi armored in a carapace of burn-blackened iron, that had trophies of Brindol's past victories bolted across all the armor. Red tattoo-like designs curled across the rest of his body.

As the leader of the Burned, Brindol was the owner of the key of the Birthing Furnace, a relic crafted by the primordial Nehushta, and thus he was responsible for the survival of the Burned genasi community as a whole. In 1469 DR, Brindol became the leader of the few Burned who survived an anaxim invasion. Under his leadership, the Burned were able to conquer the small section of Glaur known as the Dominion of the Burned.

## Brandiar

The Duchy of Brandiar was a province of Damara.

Geography
Farming settlements dominated the landscape for most of Brandiar, with a stretch of hills known as the Brandiar Moor domaining the southwest of the duchy.

Brandiar was bordered to the west by the Duchy of Arcata and to the south by the Duchy of Carmathan, with both borders marked by the arch of the Beaumaris River. The Duchy of Soravia bordered Brandiar to the east and north, with the eastern boundary marked by the Goliad River and the northern boundary by the Soravian town of Helmsdale.

Government
Brandiar was loosely ruled by a duke based in the capital city of Goliad, which sat in the east of the province. Duke Ebelard Brandebury was the last of the historic Brandebury line to rule, and after his death, the position of duke remained vacant between 1357 DR and 1359 DR. At this point, a common farmer turned war hero named Dormythyrr was appointed to the position of "Steward of the Duchy" and was soon after named Duke.

Trade
Brandair was one of Damara's "supply provinces" charged with providing the food and resources necessary to support the larger population centers further south along the Golaid River. Merchants and traders would ply the river with barges and keelboats to bring supplies to Helgabal and the other towns along Lake Mogador. During the winter months, the Goliad and Beaumaris would freeze, turning them into icy roadways that continued to carry goods on sledges pulled by horses. Goliad's central location at the junction of the Goliad and the Beumaris made it one of the most influential trade centers in Damara as much of the mineral resources out of the Galena Mountains flowed through it.

History
The duchy was ruled by the noble House Brandebury until the Vaasan War of the mid–14 century DR. When Zhengyi the Witch-King invaded Damara in the, Brandiar bore the brunt of much of the fighting, and its capital at Goliad was completely flattened over the course of the decade-long war. Eventually, Brandiar became the first Damaran province to fall to the Witch-King's armies when Duke Ebelard Brandebury surrendered. Ebelard died soon after, supposedly of a broken heart after conceding defeat, and the ruling seat was left vacant as Zhengyi preferred to leave the duchy in chaos. Ultimately, the war ended in the, with a victory for Zhengyi at the Ford of Goliad, a crossing of the Beaumaris River just west of Goliad. With this, Zhengyi purged nearly all of the ruling houses of Damara, including wiping out the remainder of House Brandebury.

Brandiar remained without a duke or even a plan for succession throughout Gareth Dragonsbane's Bloodstone Wars two years later, and was not a major player in that war. It was not until the forces of Vaasa marched again that the people of Brandiar joined in the Damaran rebellion. A common farmer named Dormythyrr assumed stewardship of Brandiar, marshaled an army, and joined Gareth Dragonsbane in the fight against Zhengyi on the fields outside of Goliad. The final battle of the Wars occurred once more at the Ford of Goliad, and once more the army of the Witch-King laid waste to the town of Goliad. However, led by Dormythyrr, the town was quickly rebuilt.

Much of the duchy was divided on the issue of supporting Gareth Dragonsbane's bid for the Damaran throne in the, with the southern areas supporting the opposing claim out of Morov and Ostel in the belief that this was a grave mistake. Attitudes began to soften when Gareth and his associate, Friar Dugald, supported the construction of a grand Church of Dionysus in Goliad. Meanwhile, Dormythyrr had earned the love and respect of the people, and it became increasingly obvious that he would be declared the next duke.

Brandiar spent many years recovering from the horrors of the Vaasan and Bloodstone Wars. The saying "eight of the ten" was common, referring to the eight years of the decade-long war that was fought largely within the duchy's borders.

Inhabitants
Brandair was sparsely populated, with most inhabitants living as farmers in scattered villages. The largest "city" was Goliad with only 250 permanent residents, although an additional 650 nearby farmers called it home on a sporadic or emergency basis. While hardy as any other Damarans, the folk of Brandiar were worn out and beaten down as of the mid-to-late 14 century DR following the wars with the Witch-King, and were eager for stability and a sense of organization in the region.

Notable Locations
 Goliad, the capital city
 Darlisan, a town on the Beaumaris River
 Dormythyrr's Ride, a road named in honor of Dormythyrr that connected Goliad with Ravensburg, the capital of the Duchy of Carmathan, to the south.

## Brazier

Braziers were simple devices that could be used to burn materials (such as incense), make a light source, or to house burners to provide a low but constant source of heat.

Description
 temple braziers,  chapel braziers, and field braziers were available for purchase through Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue. They sold gold, silver, and bronze variants of each.. The catalogue also sold special bronze braziers that came with matching burners and bronze braziers that stood atop tripod stands.

Notable Braziers
Braziers were often enchanted for the purposes of summoning and controlling
creatures, or just to create magical effects that covered large areas. Some such braziers included the following:
 Brazier of commanding fire elementals, a variety of magical braziers that allowed one to control fire elementals.
 Brazier of sleep smoke, a variety of magical braziers that looked identical to a brazier of commanding fire elementals but were actually a deadly trap.

Notable Users of Braziers
Classes
 Braziers were considered to be an essential item in any wizard's laboratory.
Magic
 A brass brazier was a required material component for the spell find familiar.
Religions
 Lolthite temples typically featured large braziers that were ornately carved with spider shapes.

## Brandon

Brandon, nicknamed "Battlemaster", was a human fighter and a renowned sword-for-hire around the Sea of Fallen Stars in the mid–14 century DR.

History
Brandon had acquired a long-running and storied reputation in the lands around the Inner Sea before 1356 DR. He had numerous exploits to his name: he destroyed the lich Tharuighagh in the hills near Saerb; he stormed the citadel of the Stonelands bandit lord Jhassilm Onespear; and he extracted the mage Rhondethar Windrider from the dungeons of Westgate, cutting through many city guards.

Activities

A master of tactics, Brandon hired himself out as a war-leader for almost any kind of action and for almost any employer, provided it did not involve seafaring and fit his moral code, and particularly if the pay was good. For his services, Brandon charged 20 gold pieces per day for himself, refunding half if the mission was a failure, and 4 gold pieces per day per man-at-arms he was required to bring along.

Although based in Priapurl on the Dragon Coast, he operated in all the lands in the Inner Sea area, as well as on the trade routes running west through the Western Heartlands to the Sword Coast, so long as he was on dry land.

Personality
Brandon was a hearty and jovial man, oft given to brawling, but this hid a very shrewd mind with a code of honor. Although a mercenary, he wouldn't do anything that would upset the peace in an area long-term, knowing that large-scale warfare would make his work much more dangerous, increase competition, and reduce funds available to pay him. He detested sea combat and all forms of seafaring, and was only ever found on dry land. He would happily tell stories of all his deeds to anyone with the patience.

Relationships
Brandon had served for a time in the Mindulgulph Mercenary Company (also based at Priapurl). However, despite greatly respecting its commander, Gayrlana, he got along poorly with her non-human troops.

Appendix
References

## Bryan the Bold

Bryan the Bold was a vassal of Edenvale in the Utter East, as well as a bloodforge mage during the time of the Bloodforge Wars (648–657 DR).

History
Bryan was a vassal of Edenvale with no noble heritage. However, he commanded a bloodforge.

When Princess Roxanna of Edenvale declared that she would only marry one who could best her in battle, Bryan was one of the men who took up the challenge. Where before Roxanna had only fought a series of weapons duels against many would-be suitors, Bryan challenged her to bloodforge battle instead, escalating the contests into a conflict known as Matchmaker Mayhem.In the Blood & Magic game, two alternate campaigns are provided, one for Roxanna and one for Bryan. These are mutually exclusive. An official history is unknown.

Bryan the Bold

As his army made camp outside Herne's Wood, Bryan issued his quite-serious challenge to Roxanna, declaring that he would invade the kingdom the next day. Roxanna therefore met him in bloodforge battle. Bryan was victorious, so Roxanna angrily and begrudgingly accepted to have him as a suitor.

However, Bryan offered Roxanna a second chance, with a second bloodforge battle to be fought in the Web Mountains. For a prize, he would hide an expensive dowry in the mountains if she in turn offered the keys to the palace. The first to get the other's prize could do as they wished without further malice. Bryan was successful, seizing Roxanna's keys.

Despite this, Bryan the Bold now turned strangely shy, declining to enter Edenvale Castle and worrying his lack of noble lineage would bring dishonor on Roxanna if she wed him. This time, it was Roxanna who made him a proposal: her father was now plotting to marry her off anyway, but she preferred Bryan to any other, and so invited him to take Edenvale Castle and seize her father's crown.

Finally, Bryan wooed Roxanna, they fell in love and were wed, ruling jointly over Edenvale, much to her father's chagrin. They had many children (at least three) as potential heirs, which worried neighboring kingdoms.

Roxanna of Edenvale
Bryan issued an ultimatum to Roxanna, threatening to invade Edenvale unless she married him. Instead, Roxanna defended her realm in a bloodforge battle outside Edenvale Castle, and defeated Bryan.

Description
Bryan the Bold had pale skin and brown hair, and typically wore studded leather armor and green breeches, with a sword.

Relations

If Bryan married Roxanna, they would have many children and potential heirs (at least three).

## Braskh

Braskh was a root found in stony areas often protected by thorn bushes in southern and southeastern Faerûn.

Description
The braskh root had a bright red skin covering a white, crisp flesh.

Uses
The uses of braskh varied depending on the amount being consumed. Lightly grated flakes of braskh were added to water used to boil vegetables, giving them a hint of spice. The Delzmaeran fiery brown gravy called sakra used braskh in more liberal quantities. Small, decoratively carved shapes of raw braskh root were set afloat in both beverages and medicinal elixirs. If a shape was chewed, most people perspired as if they had done heavy labor, cried as if they lost a loved one, and had profuse discharges from the nose and mouth. If swallowed whole, it purged the other end of the digestive tract, acting as a strong laxative.

Culture
In Delzimmer, it was usually the adolescents that ventured into the surrounding countryside with their hand tocks (small mattocks with short handles) to harvest braskh roots. One full-sized root or two smaller ones typically sold for a copper piece.

Appendix
References

## Bralatar

Bralatar was a malaugrym in the mid–14 century DR.

History
In the, Bralatar and Lorgyn managed to find a new gate to Faerûn. There, they discovered that the Great Foe of the malaugrym, Elminster, had been killed during the second battle of Shadowdale. So Bralatar and Lorgyn went to Sembia to collect magic from the wizards of that land. There they killed the wizard Mortoth and ran into their fellow malaugrym Lunquar, who was busy killing local Zhentarim. They decided to check Elminster's tower, hoping to find the secrets of Malaug. Back at the Tower of Mortoth, however, Bralatar was killed in a sudden attack by Irendue Nuen-tar, Mortoth's most promising apprentice.

Typical bracers of defense found after the Second Sundering were hollow with an intricate golden pattern.

The power of the bracers could only be accessed if the attuned wearer had no armor and no shield, but did work with other kinds of kinds of magical protection. Their magic worked by increasing the chance of attacks (magical or otherwise) to miss the wearer.

King Halvor II, an ambitious king.

## Brigitte Bannal

Brigitte Bannal was a mage and ruler of the Realm of Lands in the Utter East during the time of the Bloodforge Wars (648–657 DR).

Brigitte understood how to use the complex terrain to her strategic advantage, making nature effectively a fortress. At last, the leader of the Legendary Campaign came to the Realm of Lands to overcome these defenses and defeat her in bloodforge battle, as part of a plan to unify the war-torn Utter East through conquest.

Appendix
References

## Branta

Brantas were herbivorous ungulates common to the North.

Description
A branta had a horse-like body but with a longer, thick-muscled neck. Its hooves were surrounded by large folds of skin that spread out its weight for walking atop deep snow and for increasing its traction on slippery ice, making its feet appear like clubs in shape. The animal's snout was covered in a massive ridge of bone, which ended in two stubby nose horns.

Behavior
Brantas were timid creatures who usually bolted when faced with danger. They use their long necks for browsing for food among the high pine boughs and their nose horns for uprooting plants. They prefer to eat in places where they can scout for danger with their long necks.

Combat
If cornered, a branta could be dangerous. Rearing and turning with a skid, it could suddenly charge at its attacker, attempting to butt with its horns and fling its attacker into the air after vigorously shaking.

Ecology
They lived in many terrains except for the most rocky and forest-covered. Brantas were immune to harm from the cold, so adapted they were to their environment. In contrast, they were especially sensitive to fire and heat.

Like many animals, brantas had a highly developed sense of smell.

A group of branta was known as a hala. They also could be spotted in pairs or alone.

Uses
Branta seemed impossible to truly tame, but branta halas were sometimes walled-in by orcs or even dragons in mountain valleys. Branta meat was light-colored and tough but very nourishing.

Appendix
Trivia
In the real world, hala is a word for "clan" for the Manchu people.

References

---

## Brittleskin

Brittleskin was a spell that caused the natural armor of a creature to become hard and brittle. If that creature were struck in combat, the crystallized tissue (such as hide, bone, chitin, scales, etc) would crack and shatter, causing more permanent and often lethal damage.

References

## Brontosaurus

A brontosaurus, also known as Thunderbeast, was a massive quadruped dinosaur.

Description
Brontosauruses were so large that most predators did not dare attack them.

Behavior
These animals were not aggressive. They calmly lumbered through the jungles ignoring smaller creatures.

Combat
Brontosauruses only fought in self-defense. If necessary, they could stomp enemies and hit them with their enormous tails, which were powerful enough to even kill smaller predators.

Ecology
Brontosauruses were common to the jungles of Chult, near swamp areas and rivers. They were also known to have once roamed the lands of the North and some shamans of the Thunderbeast tribe claimed that they could still be found living within the High Forest.

Brightbuckler Street was located in Northbank. It was a relatively short street, running a full length of only about 100 yards. It began at the River Rauvin, east of the Moonbridge, and headed north. It then bent to the west and merged into the Moonway.

## Bristar

Bristar (called Velethuil by its elven inhabitants) was a village of moon elves in Deepingdale, and one of the largest community of elves in Cormanthor during the late 14 century DR. They had a strong alliance with the Dalesmen, lending their archers to the dale's militia in their shared fight against the drow.

Etymology
The name "Bristar" is a contraction of the name "Brightstar". Brightstar was the name of the clan of elves living there. The elven name for the settlement comes from their former leader, Velethuil, who was tragically killed by a forest buffalo when scouting the region to found the village.

Activities
The elves of Bristar were particularly protective of Lake Eredruie, a sacred pool of water whose waters had healing properties to elves and half-elves.

History
Following the great Retreat of the elves to the island nation of Evermeet, the elves of Bristar that chose to stay on the mainland allied themselves with Deepingdale over the Elven Court–aligned settlement of Semberholme. This decision added to the community's strength and prosperity in the wake of an overall decreased elven population.

## Bral

Bral was the sole city on the surface of the Rock of Bral and one of the greatest merchant spaceports known to any of the races of the multiverse.

Description

The city of Bral was built on the upper side of the Rock of Bral. The underside was reserved for agriculture and for government property.

The city was divided into two or three main sections, termed the High City, the Middle City, and the Low City. The High City was located on the "trailing end" of the asteroid as it traveled through wildspace. Here was located the castle of the prince. A great wall and Lake Bral separated the High City from the rest of Bral.

At the leading edge of the asteroid were the Low City and the docks. Here was where most of the spelljammers would arrive for trade. There were cavern warehouses for storing goods and hoists for lifting things up to the surface. The Low City also contained a dry dock where spelljammers designed to land on a flat surface instead of on water could park. As one passed from the Low City to the Middle City, inns and shops replaced the taverns and ale houses. As one continued on, the prince's palace could be seen beyond the greenery of the noble's villas.

Government

The city was ruled by a prince and was an unchartered monarchy. The prince could levy taxes, regulate trade, judge laws, and declare war. Typically, he would appoint bodies to enforce any changes that he made.

However, Bral also had three government councils, which hypothetically had the power to overrule the prince, though historically, they never once used this power.

The first such council was the Council of the City, which represented the people and was composed of 30 members appointed directly by the prince for lifetime service. Each Councilman represented one of the many neighborhoods of Bral or one of Bral's Underbarons. This council served as advisors to the prince and could overrule his decisions by unanimous vote.

The Noble Council was composed of all 37 chartered landholders in the city. (Except for these "nobles", the prince owned the rest of the Rock of Bral.) By majority vote, this council could remove land from any owner, except that the prince also had a vote that counted for 49%, so to remove him of his land would require a unanimous vote.

Sitting underneath this council was the Bureau of Land Distribution and Collection, which regulated the real estate market.

The final council was the Council of Captains, on which any owner of more than four spelljammers was permitted a seat if they also leased or owned land on the Rock. This council regulated trade by means of tariffs or trade rules. The prince could veto any decision made by this council, but he otherwise only had a tie-breaking vote in their decisions.

Law and Order
Piracy and slavery were both officially illegal, but charges were generally only pressed if the prince wanted to get rid of the accused. Betrayal of Bral was the ultimate crime and could include simple treason against the crown or doing something that put the whole asteroid at danger, such as starting a fire, which could deplete the air envelope. Those guilty of such a crime might be handed over to the illithid embassy to be eaten.

However, for the most part, laws were absent in Bral, and there was not even a city watch to keep order in the streets.

Trade
Bral was the epitome of free trade. Money was power, and anything and everything was bought and sold there, from a vast number of worlds and crystal spheres. Bral was full of shops and contained two major open-air markets, the Great Market and the Lesser Market, usually called the Thieves' Market. Musicians and entertainers filled the streets, hoping to earn coppers for their songs.

Coinage from at least thirty different realms was known to be accepted in Bral, but paper currency was not accepted from even the most powerful nations. Coins from other locations would need to be exchanged by the Royal Exchequer, with a 5% service fee applied. The Royal Exchequer accepted coins from at least 100 different countries.

The cost of living in Bral was much higher than in other places, and most things cost half again as much as they would on the surface of a planet. Rent for an apartment in the Low City cost anywhere from 5 to 30 gold pieces per month. A cheap room at an inn would cost about 3 gold per month, while a fancier room might be as much as 8 gold per month.

A laborer in Bral earned about 12 gold per month, while a longshoreman earned double that. The agent for a merchant might take home as much as 40 gold per month.

Four distinct groups engaged in trade in Bral: the large trading companies, who dealt in bulk materials; the smaller, familial merchant houses, who usually specialized in a unique type of cargo; small merchants, shopkeepers, and the owners of inns and taverns; and, finally, independent spelljammer captains.

Society

The city of Bral was full of rogues and unsavory characters of all kinds.
Nevertheless, everyone was polite to everyone in the city of Bral; mind flayers could be seen conversing with elves and dwarves with beholders. This was not because they were friends; rather, the city had a policy of "check your vendettas at the docks". A simple walk down any of Bral's main streets would introduce a visitor to hundreds of languages.

Humans maintained the highest population, but almost all manner of races lived here. The city contained several districts where the various races were dominant. These included:
 The Burrows, where many halflings lived;
 The Dwarven District;
 The Dracon Enclave;
 The Forest, where many elves lived;
 Giff-town; and
 Shou-town.

Also found in the city of Bral were gnomes, beholders, and mind flayers. Neogi were not welcome in Bral.

While severely overcrowded, there was little poverty in Bral, because there was always work to be found, even if it was hard work at the docks, and visiting ships very often took on new crewmembers.

The nobles of Bral were the 37 persons besides the prince who actually owned land on the small asteroid. All other lands belonged to the prince, and all other citizens rented from him or the nobles.

Religion
The city contained a number of temples, mostly located near Lake Bral. In addition to many faiths or pantheons from Toril, there were also churches for the Polygots, Pantheists, the Church of Ptah, and the Path and the Way. Nonhuman faiths also had a presence in Bral.

History
The settlement that would one day become Bral began near the start of the 13 century DR as a pirate hideout for the Black Brotherhood, whose leader was one Captain Bral. When Bral fell in an ambush to elves, the remaining pirates on the Rock named their town after him.

Over time, the population of Bral shifted, until by the end of the 13 century, there were more merchants and shopkeepers in the city, selling goods and ale to the pirates, than pirates themselves. One pirate, Captain Cozar, saw this as an opportunity. He purchased all land in the city and the rest of the asteroid from the merchants and forced any pirates to lease from him or run actual businesses if they wanted to remain. In this way, Cozar became ruler of the city above all the other pirates.

Prince Cozar ruled remarkably fairly for a former pirate, and the city thrived and grew as a legitimate center of trade, as trade turned out to be more profitable than piracy.

Prince Cozar's successor, Prince Frun, was not as good a ruler as his father. Nevertheless, the city continued growing from its trade, as companies from at least twelve different crystal spheres established businesses there. Frun commissioned the building of the Citadel and the Donjon and rebuilt and expanded the royal palace.

At one point during Frun's 35-year reign, he had insulted a neogi trader, which triggered an attack from a neogi fleet set on razing the city. They succeeded in destroying a part of the Middle City by crashing an abandoned dwarven citadel into it, but then retreated.

After Frun died, Prince Calar ruled for only six days before being assassinated by being jettisoned into space. His brother, Prince Andru, then took the throne. Within fifteen years, Andru had doubled the size of Bral's army, tripled the size of its navy, and strengthened the law-enforcement of the government.

Notable Locations

The city contained a graveyard, although most of the dead were cremated, composted, or set adrift in wildspace.

Rumors & Legends
The older and more superstitious citizens of the city believed that Bral would one day be destroyed by the asteroid's original inhabitants.

## Braunstar Wheelwright

Braunstar Wheelwright was a wagon and carriage shop in Ashabenford in the late 1300's DR. It was run by the expert wainwright Elmo Braunstar, a wise and beloved man within Mistledale, who designed, sold and repaired wagons and carriages of all kinds.

Services
The shop offered extremely reasonable prices, so much so that traveling merchants passing through would often purchase a pair of wheels to ensure an even axle in case of a wheel break. As a result the shop was often low on wheels for sale.

Products
The shop featured a number of carriages and wagons of Braunstar's design, including the Swanswing covered carriage, which was very popular among nobles, and the Fleetrun long wagon, which were a prideful means of conveyance for the merchants of the Dragon Reach. The latter were so popular they had a three-year-long back order, to the point where they were the target of thieves even when traveling empty.

## Brondeth

Brondeth of the Broken Staff was a human mage.

Alongside Auziiyra Twelvestars and several other mages, Brondeth was guarding a human mining community when they were attacked by drow. The mages used the wonderform spell to hide in the form of wingless wonders and escape being killed, but became lost in the Underdark. They were thought to still be wandering the Night Below by the mid–14 century DR.

## Brotherhood of the Moon

The Brotherhood of the Moon was an adventuring company of Selûne-faithful, based in the Icespear House, within the village of Deadsnows, during the years leading up to 1385 DR. They were affiliated with the Knights in Silver,Shades of Blue Fire refers to them as Knights of Silver. the elite defenders of Silverymoon and Luruar.

Headquarters
While the Brotherhood did take up residence in the Icespear House for a time, their inner sanctum was hidden beneath a magically sealed slab on the manor's stone floor. For over a hundred years, the sanctum was protected by the Brotherhood's guardians, an eidolon and a pair of trap haunts.

History
Shortly before the Spellplague of 1385 DR, the Brotherhood settled into the town of Deadsnows and wound up taking residence within the Icespear House. From there, they began patrolling the region in order to defend it from the malevolent raiders of the Nether Mountains.

The brotherhood began to garner some considerable wealth from their exploits. When the blue fire came upon the Icespear House once again, it quickly killed many within the group. Those few that survived were forced to abandon their cache of riches and magic items, hidden within their inner sanctum.

## Brightvigil

Brightvigil was a potion that protected against sleep-inducing magical effects.

Description
Brightvigil was a light, clear liquid made by the sorceress and alchemist Angrath Dree from herbs and saps found in northwest Faerûn. It could be purchased from her at Old Angrath's Smokehouse in Red Larch, or possibly from secondary vendors elsewhere. The usual price for a single dose was 30 gp.

Powers
The effects of brightvigil lasted one hour. During that hour the imbiber was much less likely to be affected by sleep spells and similar magics.

## Brokengulf

The Brokengulf family  of Waterdeep was a wealthy mercantile house that had been a part of the city's nobility for over a hundred years as of the mid–14 century DR. They were renowned explorers and hunters who were known for tracking and capturing wild beasts.

Base of Operations

The family owned a villa in the Sea Ward, on the south side of Wrightstone Street between Diamond Street and Mendever Street.

History
House Brokengulf was ennobled in the  when the Lords of Waterdeep recognized the various merchant families as nobility within the city and its realm.

The Brokengulfs nearly went bankrupt after losing a great amount of their wealth to the ancient green dragon, Claugiyliamatar, following an elaborate ruse she played on the family's patriarch, Lord Saerlin. When she confronted Saerlin with blackmail, and he in turn sought out a dragonslayer, the great wyrm collapsed the tower of the Brokengulf villa upon him, crippling the lord and killing three of his sisters.

Following the death of Lord Morus Brokengulf I, Morus II returned from the Sword Coast to the family's estate in the Sea Ward to assume his role as the head of House Brokengulf. After a few months of frustration in this role, he turned over operation of the family's business to his friend and fellow Knight, Sir Gareth Cormaeril. When Lady Aridarye Phylund married Prendergast Brokengulf in the, she became the family's matriarch and shared control of the family's interests with Sir Gareth.

Members
 Aridarye Brokengulf: The family's de facto leader.
 Forstag Brokengulf: The grand uncle of Morus Brokengulf II who wielded a Scalesbane and died while exploring the Mere of Dead Men.
 Morus Brokengulf I: The deceased patriarch of House Brokengulf.
 Morus Brokengulf II: A paladin of Tyr within the Holy Order of the Knights of Samular who spent most of his time away from home.
 Prendergast "Gast" Brokengulf: Husband of Aridarye and uncle of Morus II.
 Saerlin Brokengulf: A deceased patriarch of the family.

## Brass

Brass was an alloy of copper and zinc, widely used for decorative and practical purposes.

Properties

Brass was commonly made from a mixture of 80% copper and 20% zinc, making 'red brass', or 70% copper and 30% zinc, making 'yellow brass'. Red brass weighed  per, while yellow brass weighed a little less at . Brass had a melting point between  and .

Availability

 Aurora's Emporium had a large catalogue of brass items from all throughout Faerun. It included a stock of plain brass materials in the form of  bars (1 sp),  ingots (3 sp),  square sheets (1 sp),  by  strips (3 cp), and  of wire (4 cp), as well as brass cylinders and dust intended for use in religious rituals and spellcasting.
 Hillmarch in Cormyr was a center of brasscasting industry, as well as the mining of the copper and zinc used in it. It produced and exported fine brasswork used across the realm.
 Hornshield keep in the Storm Horns contained a small but proficient brasscasting, glassblowing, and ironworking industry.
 Ember Me More, a specialty store in Ravens Bluff, sold a wide variety of fireplace-related products made from brass.
 Lamps by Watt, another specialty store in Ravens Bluff, specialized in constructing light fixtures and various other items from brass.
 Brass was one of the major exports for the Lake of Steam region.

Usages
Currency
 The Waterdhavian taol was an unusual square brass coin, worth 2 gp in Waterdeep, but nothing elsewhere.
 Gond bells were small brass bells typically worth 10 gp each, though valued at 20 gp by temples of Gond.

Mundane Items

 Common, everyday objects were often made of brass, such as keyrings, pins, lamps, chandeliers, thuribles, ornaments, tools, scales, needles, spyglasses, animal cages, censers, bells, door knockers, candlesticks. water faucets, and barrel spigots.
 Diningware was often made from brass as a cheaper alternative to pewter and silver, including mugs, steins, tankards, knives, forks, and spoons.
 Some Calishite lamps and kaleidoscopes were known to be constructed from brass.
 Brass was one of a variety of metals that hook-hands and peg-legs were typically constructed from.
 The mechanisms for timepieces and music boxes were often housed in brass casings.
 Brass was one of three metals that the spinning tops of Amn were known to be constructed with.
 Brass was one of three metals that hurricane lamps were known to be constructed with. The craftfolk of Dapplegate were particularly known for crafting brass stormlanterns.
 Inexpensive jewellery and ornamentation could be made from brass, such as decorative pins, hairpins, rings, and chokers.
 Brass was commonly used as a fitting or decoration in other projects, such as bookcases, mapcases, buckles for shoes and clothing, and books.
 A wide variety of musical horns were typically constructed of brass, including fanfare horns, glaurs, hunting horns, longhorns, and sackbutts. Other musical brass instruments included wargongs, tockens, and chime lamps.
 The Crown of Cormyr dispensed Court tokens to grant the right to bear arms. The token consisted of a numbered brass plaque engraved with the Purple Dragon symbol.
 A special hull sheathing that protected ship timber from shipworms and seaweed growth used brass and other metals.
 Although mundane weapons and armor were rarely made of brass, it was a common decorative element, and some items could be plated or sheathed in brass.
 Oscray forged chainmail, as well as many of their weapons, from brass.
 Cultists who worshiped Baphomet were known to decorate their altars with brass weapons.
 The holy symbol of Gond was a toothed cog made of brass, bronze, iron, or bone.

Construction
 In Memnonnar, the domes and minarets that topped the buildings were inlaid with brass.
 The Twin Towers of the Eternal Eclipse had several alarm bells throughout the complex that were made of brass.
 The bricks of Gilgeam's Ziggurat of Eternal Victory were decorated with blue enamel, brass, gold, silver, and other precious metals.
 The Great Brass Gate was the portal though which the noble efreeti Memnon entered the world of Toril to found Memnonnar.
 The Grand Cemetary of Elturel was surrounded by a spiked brass fence and gate.

Other Planes

 Boats made of brass were used by efreet to traverse the Sea of Fire.
 The City of Brass made heavy use of this metal. One notable building in the city to utilize this material was the Charcoal Palace.
 Azer were often known to wear apron-like garments made from beaten brass.
 Celestial archons, with the exception of lantern archons, wore armor made of brass to indicate that they had reached the third rank within the archon hierarchy.
 Orthons were known to carry crossbows made of brass.
 The Bleeding Citadel had large double doors of brass.
 Ember guards were made of brass and stone.

Magic Items

 Spell focuses could be made of brass.
 The spellbooks Arcanabula of Jume and Detho's Libram were both bound in brass rings.
 Braziers of commanding fire elemental were constructed of brass.
 Efreeti bottles were usually constructed of brass.
 Eversmoking bottles were usually constructed of brass.
 The Farseer of Illusk was partially made of brass.
 Harvyn's Ring
 Helms of underwater action were forged from brass.
 Some Horns of Valhalla were constructed of brass.
 Lightbringer was an enchanted mace made entirely of brass.
 Rings of dragons were brass rings highly prized by members of the Cult of the Dragon.
 Rings of flying were made with brass lining the edges.
 Peacemaker's rods had sculpted brass ends.
 Purple Dragon rings were always crafted from brass.
 The serpent rings worn by worshipers of serpent and reptilian deities were fashioned from brass.
 Staffs of entrapment were traditionally made by the Netherese with brass.
 The wooden Quarterstaff of Alcedor had brass covers on each end.
 Countess Zaranda Star's  talking head statue was made of brass.
 Clockroaches were usually made of brass or bronze.

Spell Components
 Early versions of Bigby's clenched fist required a pair of brass knuckles for casting the spell.
 Call of despair required a small brass gong for casting the spell.
 Darsson's music box required a metal cube for casting the spell and brass was one of the two metals that could be used.
 Detect scrying required a miniature brass hearing trumpet for casting the spell.
 Dreamspeak required a bell made of either brass or silver for casting the spell.
 Find familiar required a brass brazier for casting the spell.
 Horizikaul's versatile vibration required a hollow brass cone as a focus.
 Hornung's surge selector required a brass spinner for casting the spell.
 Brass tuning forks were required when using a plane shift spell to enter the Para-Elemental Plane of Magma.
 Undetectable lies required a handful of brass dust for casting the spell.
 A brass bell was one of two options for material component when casting the spell Watchware.

History
 The Lodestar Mountains of ancient Netheril supplied the realm with great amounts of various ores, including native brass. The mines were abandoned as of  and infested with monsters.
 The city of Ulshantir in Asram was famous for its copperworking and brasscasting.
 A brass coffin was used for the burial of Theldrin Rallyhorn.

## Brunhilde Erikksonn

Brunhilde Erikksson was the prop-and-costume-master for the Ravens Bluff Playhouse, as well as an officer within the city's bards' guild during the late 14 century DR. She acted as mother figure to the younger bards and performers within the guild, helping through their various artistic, romantic or existential crises as they arose.

The experienced performer was considered a master actor, dancer and singer among her peers. She was proficient in speaking both dwarvish as well elven.

Activities
The cellars of the city Playhouse served as the domain of prop-master Erikksonn. Within the dusty, old maze of tunnels, Brunhilde maintained a wealth of props and costumes including various stage-size wagons, rowboats, and even a halved sailing ship, various suits of armor (some of which were real, but most were boiled leather painted silver), collapsible Pole arms, shields with brightly-painted, fanciful heraldry, a prop shanty house, livestock pen and even a privy.

Description
Brunhilde was a particularly large woman, whose broad, round face was highlighted with florid cheeks. Her resting expression was something of a contradiction; her sharp, twinkling gray eyes starkly contrasted with her near-permanent frown, that made her seem as if she were in a state of constant disapproval. She wore her long, gray hair in a distinct singular braid.

History
Years ago, Brunhilde Erikksonn was renowned singer within Ravens Bluff, toasted at the revels of noble houses and pursued by scores of adoring men. While she has gracefully retired without remonstration, she still occasionally sought the company of older Ravenian nobles, who fondly remembered her youthful beauty and exceptional talent, for a night of drinks and flirtation.

Brester Orthwood was the most competent son of House Orthwood, a fairly new noble house in Cormyr circa 1372 DR.

Personality
He was fairly spontaneous in personality, and would gallop wildly into whatever his latest enthusiasm was. Crown agents kept a close eye on him to see if he would make something of himself.

Abilities
He was a competent fighter, and this led him to getting noticed by agents of the Crown.

## Bracer of flying daggers

A bracer of flying daggers was a magic armband that provided its wearer with an endless supply of single-use, magical daggers.

Description
The bracer had the appearance of being adorned with thin daggers strapped to it.

Powers
An attuned wearer could pull two daggers from the bracer simultaneously at any time, which disappeared if not immediately thrown. The thrown daggers remained solid while in flight and when they either hit or missed a target, after which they also immediately disappeared.

Notable Owners
The mercenary Jarlaxle Baenre used bracers of flying daggers. He had several as part of his extensive collection of magic items, including the Blades Injurious.

## Brotherhood of the Sun

The Brotherhood of the Sun was a monastic order dedicated to Amaunator.

Organization
The Brotherhood of the Sun's membership consisted of monks. In fact, all monks who received their spells from Amaunator were part of it.

Activities
The monks' duties included work at a temple dedicated to Amaunator like any other of his priests. However, the monks were a bit of an exception for they were exempted from it from time to time for their special duties, namely field work. They intermingled with the general populace of ancient Netheril and reassured them with the edicts of the Yellow God and ensured order among them.

Tactics
The monks of Amaunator were spellcasting priests but were not granted the ability to turn undead like the clerics and sunlords, the latter being Amaunator's specialty priests.

Base of Operations
As mentioned above, the monks were temporarily exempt from temple duties and had their own duties. However, they were still attached to a temple of Amaunator and had to make at least annual reports to the temple they were part of.

Relationships
The Brotherhood of the Sun was part of the church of Amaunator. They were also the forebears of the Order of the Sun Soul.

History
The Brotherhood of the Sun was active during the days of ancient Netheril as a monastic order dedicated to Amaunator. They survived the death of both. The former happened in, the latter happened a thousand years after that.

After the death of Amaunator, the monks stopped being a uniform force. Each monastery chose a new deity for itself, mostly Lathander, then Selûne, and then Sune. They became the Order of the Sun Soul.

References

## Brassberg

Brassberg was one of the divine realms of the draconic deity Hlal the Jester, who was usually known as Aasterinian in the Outer Planes.

Cosmography
In the Great Wheel cosmology, Brassberg was Hlal's lair deep within Ysgard's third layer, Nidavellir (her other realm was a secret lair in Arborea).The 3rd-edition Core book Races of the Dragon, still using the Great Wheel cosmology, treated Hlal and Aasterinian as distinct entities and placed the former in Arborea, the latter in the Outlands.

In the World Tree cosmology, the realm covered part of the lower slopes of the great Dragon Eyrie.Hlal's realm was not named in the Player's Guide to Faerûn. This article assumes that it is the same as Brassberg.

Description
The lair of Hlal consisted of a number of caverns, with just the space to contain the riches collected by the dragon goddess. The surrounding area was dry and warm, as befitted the patron of brass and copper dragons.

Features
A portal linked Brassberg to Erevan Ilesere's realm on the plane of Arvandor.

## Brarindra Harbright

Brarindra Harbright was one of the proprietors of the newly opened Roaring Dragon House just outside of Waterdeep circa the Year of Wild Magic, 1372 DR.

Abilities
Brarindra was quite skilled at running a large gambling hall operation. On her shifts she oversaw the many escorts, servers, coin keepers (people who ran the games), tellers, and over forty mercenary guards at the Roaring Dragon.

Activities
She had many duties during the day-to-day operation of a popular night spot. One of them was acquiring beasts and monsters to battle in the pit for the crowd's entertainment. It was known in some circles that she had a standing offer of 600 gold pieces for any unintelligent, healthy monsters that were able to put up a good fight for the clientele.

Relationships
Brarindra was the niece of Drengar Harbright, proprietor of the High Flagon inn and gambling hall, one of the other Harbright family businesses. He had the Roaring Dragon House built and put her and her brother, Thoalur, in charge. They took turns working twelve-hour shifts, occasionally relieved by other members of the Harbright clan.

## Broxley Fairkettle

Broxley Fairkettle was a strongheart halfling representative of the Fellowship of Innkeepers in Waterdeep in the early 1490s DR.

History
In 1492 DR, Broxley possibly went to check the new owners of the Trollskull Manor, trying hard to recruit them into his guild and offering advice.

Personality
Broxley always lamented the difficulty of being the father of nine children and believed that if all followed the Guild's rules his life would become little easier.

## Brux

Brux was the second layer of the Beastlands. A realm locked in perpetual twilight, it contained the divine realms of Deneir, Milil, Haela Brightaxe, and the cat lord.

Geography
The sun appeared as a red orb always touching the horizon of Brux, while a half moon could be seen hanging low on the opposite side of the sky. Other seers believed that there were two suns (and no moon), with each of them located on opposite sides of the horizon. It was considerably cooler than Krigala, so fog and mist commonly rose from the ground and obscured the trees. All trees grew slanted on the layer, pointing toward the dim source of light of the perpetually setting sun, and created long shadows. This seemingly frozen state could be disconcerting to visitors, but it did not disturb the activity cycle of the animals that lived there.

The layer had an abundance of creeks and streams that led to Krigala and eventually flowed into the Oceanus.

Notable Locations
 The Cat Lord's Prowl, realm of the cat lord.
 Findar Endar, realm of Haela Brightaxe.
 The Library of All Knowledge, shared realm of Deneir and Milil.
 Ursis, realm of Balador.

Inhabitants
The layer's main inhabitants were animals whose activity cycles peaked around dawn and dusk, such as tigers, bats, and foxes.

Ferrix, the playful goddess of weretigresses, had no permanent realm but roamed twilight Brux freely.

References

Connections

## Brilliant energy arrow

Brilliant energy arrow was a transmutation spell that changed the properties of an arrow or bolt.

Effects
Brilliant energy arrow was a spell that could only be cast on an arrow or bolt of masterful quality touched by the caster. The enchanted projectile's head started to shed light like a torch. Its other property was an inability to cause harm to lifeless matter like undead, constructs, and objects in general. This came with the ability to pass through armor, effectively ignoring its defenses. Put simply, the spell gave the powers of a brilliant energy weapon for its duration. Said duration was very short. The caster had only enough time to cast the spell and shoot it within the six seconds after its casting as the magic dissipated after that time.

Components
Brilliant energy arrow required verbal and material components. The latter was the arrow or bolt of masterful quality on which the spell was cast.

## Brazhal Kos

Brazhal Kos was a powerful enchanter and a member of the Red Wizards of Thay during the 14 century DR.

History
For many years, the nobles of Thay led hunting parties out on the waters of Lake Thaylambar in an effort to kill some of the great dragon turtles that inhabited its icy depths. While this was extremely dangerous, and often led to disaster, Brazhal Kos developed a means to actually enslave the famed beasts in the year 1371 DR.

Kos created a magical harness that bent the creature's will to his own and granted him dominion over its actions. He then offered his services to Homen Odesseiron, the tharchion of Surthay, likely to aid the ruler's efforts at military control over Lake Mulsantir in his constant struggles with Rashemen to the north.

Breathe was an alteration spell of the southern magic tradition.
Effects

In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a material component. A cloth of any material was held over the caster's mouth while reciting the verbal component of the spell.

## Brawling Hands

The Brawling Hands were a pair of gauntlets of dexterity that were believed to have been crafted in the lands of Kara-Tur.

Powers
These gauntlets greatly increased the agility of the wearer, assuming they didn't already possess heroic-level reflexes.

History
There were legends that stated the Brawling Hands were worn a master of the martial arts who brought them to Faerûn after a long journey from the Far East.

While it was not exactly known how these magical gauntlets first arrived on the Sword Coast they were seen in the possession of an ogrillon named Hairtooth. The half-orc/ogre regularly extorted travelers at a bridge crossing with his comrade Gnarl during the mid-14 century DR.

## Bracers to the death

The bracers to the death were a specific set of bracers of defense that circulated around Faerûn during the 14 century DR.

Powers
These magical bracers greatly increased the defenses of the wearer, assuming they were wearing no armor other than simple clothes.

History
The bracers once belonged to a gladiator of an unknown land, who wore them while fighting in the arena for decades. While he was undefeated for years, he was finally killed the day that the bracers to the death went missing.

During the 1360's, rumors circulated that they were still worn by those who engaged in feats of competitive combat. In truth they had been in the possession of the mage Davaeorn, who had been working as an agent of the Iron Throne within the mines beneath the Cloak Wood forest.

By the Year of the Gauntlet, 1369 DR they had come into the possession of another wizard Mekrath, who had imprisoned the tiefling performer Haer'Dalis beneath the Temple District of Athkatla.

Notable Owners
Elan Garaq, a rakshasa trader in the Black Pits.
Dulf Ebonbeard, Gerrold Darkfellow, and Gezzthemin from Dennaton's gladiatorial Thayvian arena.

Broargar "Windcloak" Iringil was a Impilturan wizard and instructor of the Art who lived in the city of Lyrabar during the 14 century DR.

Activities
Iringil was the founder as well as one of the head teachers of the Towers of the Wind, a well-known magical academy located within his home city. He was quite proud of the school he had established. Although it was regarded as rather small, compared to some other arcane institutions that were found throughout Faerûn, it was considered the finer magical school of Impiltur.

## Break limb

Break limb was an alteration spell that allowed a cleric to break the limb of another creature by mere touch alone. The reverse of this spell was called mend limb and caused the bones of a broken appendage to successfully bind back together.

Effects
While in effect, the caster gained the ability to potentially fracture, snap, or break the limb of the next being they touched. While it was possible they could resist the effects, the touch was still harmful nonetheless.

Depending on which bone was broken, the recipient of the broken limb suffered reduced capabilities until the bone was properly healed. This could be accomplished by a month of bedrest, a successful casting of mend limb, or heal.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a small broken bone taken from any animal.

## Brevindon Margaster

Brevindon Margaster was a member of House Margaster in the late 15 century DR.

History
In 1488 DR, Regis was informed by one of his informants that Brevindon Margaster had gone to the North during winter and stopped at a stable near the Driftwood Tavern near Neverwinter.

Some time later, Brevindon Margaster headed for the Sword Coast with a pirate flotilla composed of humans, goblinoids, and even gnolls with the objective to attack Luskan. Brevindon's fleet easily sacked Port Llast.

After his takeover of Luskan, Brevindon proclaimed himself the new High Captain of Ship Kurth and all the others High Captains submitted to him. Brevindon took residence inside the Ship Kurth compound but Wulfgar, Kimmuriel Oblodra, and Bonnie Charlee planned to infiltrate the place and kill Brevindon and Asbeel.

Brevindon and Asbeel were fused together and while Kimmuriel provided psychic support, Wulfgar engaged the man/demon and by surprise Bonnie threw away the demonic phylactery that the barbarian destroyed. Thanks to this, Kimmuriel managed to banish Asbeel from Brevindon's body and the exhausted man surrendered.

Relationship
Brevindon was the brother of Lady Inkeri Margaster. He housed inside himself the demonic phylactery of the cambion Asbeel and they worked in tandem. He idolized Lord Dagult Neverember.

The Breeding Pen was a festhall located in the southwestern Ring Caverns of Oryndoll. This was a place where mind flayers went to breed their prize thralls.

Other illithid would often watch the thralls breed and actively read the thrall's minds during the act. The purpose was to feel what they normally could not, since they were sexless.

The Brain Brokerage was located on the edge of the southwestern-most section of the Ring Caverns of Oryndoll. The mind flayer merchants of the consortium often specialized in acquiring rare brains from willing merchants of other races.

It was common for the brain brokerage to organize caravans to other Underdark cities with the purpose of getting access to rare brains. Caravans traveled to Guallidurth, Ooltul, Sloopdilmonpolop, Sshamath, and Zokir.

## Broadhand

Broadhand was a blacksmith, owner of Broadhand's Forge and Tack, and a high-ranking member of the Church of Bane.

Description
The Banite man wore a metal vest over studded leather armor. He also wore a green beret, gauntlets, and wielded a quarterstaff.

History
Circa 1356 DR, an acolyte of Bane reported to Broadhand. The acolyte had managed to intercept a letter written by a wizard, Amelior Amanitas. The wizard had learned a lot about a Zhentarim plot from an escaped slave, which of course was of interest to the Banites, who were historical enemies of the Zhentarim.

Broadhand began writing his own letter to the Banite temple in Nesmé about the new information he had uncovered. However, no messenger replied (as the Heroes of Ascore-to-be had slain him), and Broadhand became very worried.

These same heroes arrived at the blacksmith's shop, and convinced him that they were the Banite priests from Nesmé. The relieved blacksmith led the heroes, whom he believed were the messengers, back to the shrine in his hidden back room.

He told the group that the Zhentarim planned to send armies across the Anauroch desert, and were close to reclaiming the fallen city of Ascore. The heroes were also informed that the Zhentarim had allied with the Kraken Society, and there were strange goings-on in Llorkh. Broadhand believed that the Banites had to act now to stop their historical enemies, and so he sent the adventurers on a quest to meet with the man with the information, Amelior Amanitas.

## Broadhand's Forge and Tack

Broadhand's Forge and Tack was a weapons and armor shop located to the south west of Silverymoon. It secretly housed an altar room in dedication to Bane.

Description
The shop was owned by the blacksmith Broadhand, and sold a large variety of items. It had a horseshoe-shaped sign, made of wood, right above the door.

The shop actually had a secret door at the back that led to several more rooms, which were used in worship to the Black Lord.

History
Circa 1358 DR, the future Heroes of Ascore came to the shop to inquire about the festival going on in Mielikki's Glade. The party convinced the owner, Broadhand, that they had been sent from Nesmé on behalf of the Banite cause. They were led through a secret door to the back section of the shop, and came before a shrine to the Black Lord. The room was decorated with statues and images of Bane, and it was here that Broadhand told them of the Zhentarim plan to seize territory across the northern realms.
