## Brain lock

Brain lock was a psionic power of the telepathy discipline. It prevented the victim from undertaking any mental actions.

Effects
If the target lacked the will to resist, their higher mind was locked away and they became dazed and mentally paralyzed, unable to actively perform any actions, even purely mental ones. They couldn't move or attack, nor make decisions, nor cast spells or use psionics. A flying creature, unable to beat its wings, was forced to descend and land, while a swimming creature was forced to stop, and an air-breathing one to head to the surface. However, they could still react and defend themselves against any physical assaults. This made other forms of mental control redundant, as the subject could not move or act even if commanded by another.

It affected one humanoid up to 130 feet (40 meters) away, or longer for more capable manifesters, and lasted as long as the manifester concentrated, plus several seconds. An experienced manifester could augment it with additional psionic energy to affect other types of creatures, but could not change the number or range.

Displays
Manifesting it had the standard visual and material displays.

Users
Brain lock could be learned by telepaths and ardents and divine minds who bore the Corruption and Madness mantle. Brain lock could also be used innately by the psionic demons called cerebriliths.

Notable Users
 Saryndalaghlothtor, "Lady Gemcloak", a crystal dragon

## Brinshaar

Brinshaar was a human magic-user that lived in Neverwinter.

Description
The mage had medium-length brown hair, deep green eyes, and wore a purple and gold scarf, as well as a green robe. He also wore several pieces of jewelry. Brinshaar was versed in fighting with darts, as well as a quarterstaff, and was very tough and robust for a mage.

Personality
The man seemed friendly, and considered himself an expert magic-user, but had bold intentions.

History

Circa 1358 DR, Brinshaar made his way into the Host Tower of the Arcane to steal a magic statuette. However, he was captured and tortured, and he claimed the Host Tower mages tried to use him to get information out about Lord Nasher's magical defenses. His captors chained him to a wall and instructed owlbears to maim him. The Heroes of Ascore-to-be made their way to the tower, saved him from the pack of owlbears, and Brinshaar offered to join with the heroes, as well as giving them information on the statuettes they most desired.

Once they had made it out of the tower, the heroes were turned on by the seemingly friendly Brinshaar. He controlled a foursome of displacer beasts to try and wrestle back the statuette. However, the traitor was defeated by the heroes, and they kept their hold on the artifact.

## Brielbara

Brielbara was a human wizard living in Baldur's Gate on the Sword Coast in the mid–14 century DR. She was one of the most notable mages in the city circa 1358 DR. She was known for developing many new spells.

Relations
She was the daughter of the Archmage Briel. In her library, she had her father's personal memoirs, A Life Lived in the Shadows of Death.

She was once married to the mage Yago, but had a brief affair with the adventurer Coran, the result of which was a half-elf daughter, Namara. Yago was enraged, but Coran did not know of his daughter.

History
In the, Yago cursed Namara, leaving her dying. Brielbara sought aid from mercenaries to steal Yago's Book of Curses from his room at the Low Lantern, so she could lift the curse.

## Briel

Briel, known as the Archmage Briel, was a human wizard of the 14 century DR.

History
Briel was once an apprentice studying under the notorious lich Aumvor the Undying. He last trained with Aumvor in the .

He once made plans to found a school of wizardry in Baldur's Gate, under the subtle guidance of Aumvor. The result was Briel's School of the Arcane, but it too ultimately failed some time before 1374 DR.Champions of Ruins page 176 says Aumvor's plan to create schools of wizardry was "largely" a "spectacular failure", suggesting Briel's School of the Arcane failed somehow. No such school has been mentioned in sources on Baldur's Gate.

Relations
He was the father of Brielbara, a wizardess of Baldur's Gate known for developing many new spells around 1358 DR.

Publications
Briel's personal memoirs were titled A Life Lived in the Shadows of Death. Notably covering his time with Aumvor, the little that was known about the Undying One after the fall of Netheril was written down in these memoirs. They were kept in Brielbara's library in Baldur's Gate by 1374 DR.

The untitled wizard's workbook known traditionally as Briel's Book of Shadows was believed to be authored by Briel, written while studying under Aumvor. Some time before 1356 DR, the sage Ubergast observed that the informal handwriting in the book matched that in a letter from Briel stating his plan to found a school of wizardry in Baldur's Gate, preserved in the city's archive.

In his writings, Briel recorded the secret of the creation of living zombies—living beings cruelly reduced to a mindless, almost undead state and favored servitors of Aumvor the Undying—but Briel never created any himself.

Notes

## Briel's School of the Arcane

Briel's School of the Arcane was a school of wizardry in Baldur's Gate on the Sword Coast.

It was founded by the Archmage Briel, who'd written a letter to the city stating his plan to start a school of wizardry there; this was preserved in the city's archive. However, this was done at the guidance of the lich Aumvor the Undying, as part of his plan to revive Netheril's spirit of spellcasting, arcane research, and experimentation in Netherese bloodlines, after previous efforts in the Brotherhood of the Black Hand in Ascalhorn and Ulcaster's School of Conjuration in Beregost failed spectacularly. The result was Briel's School of the Arcane, but it too ultimately failed some time before 1374 DR.Champions of Ruins page 176 says Aumvor's plan to create schools of wizardry was "largely" a "spectacular failure", suggesting Briel's School of the Arcane failed somehow, but its actual fate is unknown. No such school has been mentioned in sources on Baldur's Gate.

## Brotherhood of the Black Hand

The Brotherhood of the Black Hand, also known as the Brothers of the Black Hand was a cadre of evil wizards originally based in Ascalhorn in the High Forest. They worshiped Bane, whose title and symbol was a black hand.

History
The Brotherhood was established in Ascalhorn shortly after the fall of Myth Drannor, at the guidance of the lich Aumvor the Undying, as part of his plan to revive Netheril's spirit of spellcasting, arcane research, and experimentation in Netherese bloodlines.

In 765 DR their existence was discovered by the Ascalhi authorites and exiled from the city. A year later they resurfaced in Silverymoon, after construction of the High Palace had begun, and placed spies among the builders. With this, they managed to steal a number of invaluable and powerful magical items and artifacts, including the collection of first High Mage Ecamane Truesilver, the glove of Taarnahm the Vigilant, Tasmia's necklace, and the staff of Silverymoon of the current High Mage, Ederan Nharimlur. The Brothers were discovered later that year and the majority were caught and executed, but the stolen treasures were not recovered.

The surviving members of the brotherhood fled back to Ascalhorn, later becoming embroiled in the escalating conflicts and power plays among the city's wizards. Their safehouse in Ascalhorn would fall to the machinations of the wizards and devils there, with their last surviving members dying in 856 DR, and the treasures therein were seized by those who killed the last of the Black Hand, before the city became Hellgate Keep in the following century. The only thing of theirs to go undiscovered was a hidden underground vault, later known as the Crypt of the Black Hand, where their bones and treasures apparently remained by the mid–14 century DR.

Appendix
References

Breathsense was a spell unique to Maztica.

Effects
This spell allowed a caster to detect the presence of any nearby breathing creatures, large or small. As the caster moved, they were able to detect new creatures as they came into range of the spell. This spell also allowed the caster to determine the approximate size of the detected creatures.

Components

## Brachiosaurus

Brachiosauruses  were a type of large and incredibly heavy dinosaur, similar to a brontosaurus.

Description
Brachiosaurs were considered to be among some of the heaviest dinosaurs to ever live, weighing around . Their bodies were around  in length.

Reputation
Though peaceful, due to their immense size brachiosaurs had a tendency to accidentally step on smaller creatures and thus could pose a threat to adventurers.

Ecology
Diet
Brachiosaurs were a herbivorous species of dinosaur.
Habitats
These creatures were known to inhabit lakes and swamps in regions with a warm climate.

## Brain mole

Brain moles were a type of mole-like animal that fed on psionic activity, and that would attack victims by psionically burrowing into their brains.

Description
Brain moles were only  long and resembled ordinary moles.

Behavior
Brain moles were only dangerous in that they needed to eat psionic energy once a week or die; they did not attack except to defend themselves. In fact, brain moles were easily tamed and friendly as far as wild animals went.

Abilities
Brain moles had some minor psionic abilities in the form of mindlink, mindwipe, contact, mind thrust, psychic drain, and psionic sense.

However, if they fed upon psionic energy from a creature that was not naturally psionic, but instead using psionic energy by way of spells or items, there was a chance that the brain mole's feeding could cause insanity.

Ecology
Brain moles lived in tunnel complexes, the only signs of which aboveground were mounded earth. They fed upon psionic energy from other creatures, but not lethally so. Brain moles typically lived in family groups consisting of a mated pair and their children; in places frequented by psionic creatures groups of families had been reported dwelling together.

Some people kept brain moles as pets; they were especially favored by royalty and nobles who had been harrassed by psionicists. Some sages even claimed that even a dead brain mole would offer some protection against psionic attacks, provided it was worn around the neck as a medallion. Adult brain moles sold for 50 gp, and young for 5 gp, on the open market.

Relationships
Brain moles were known to serve the duergar deities Laduguer and Deep Duerra.

## Bracer

A bracer was a piece of armor that protected the wrist and forearm. Bracers most often came in pairs, and magical ones usually only imparted their power when both were worn.

Description
Materials that made up bracers tended to vary, from leather to metal to truesilver. They were not only used as armor but also as weapons, as some bracers were modified to have claws.

Powers
Bracers were often enchanted, mostly to provide protection or to improve skill at tasks requiring great dexterity, such as archery.

Some rare and wondrous magical bracers were able to function alone, such as Jarlaxle's bracer of flying daggers.

 Priests of Malar, cultists who wore spiked bracers

## Brown Horse

The Brown Horse was a tavern in Thundertree in the 15 century DR.

Location
The tavern was located to the northwest of the village, close to its entrance.

Structure
Its exterior was well-kept, even after the eruption of Mount Hotenow in 1451 DR. The intact, stony wall had a sign close to the entry door of a horse holding a tankard of ale.

The building was split into two sections: its eastern side housed the common room, whilst its western side housed the brewing canisters and kitchen.

Services
The Brown Horse was well known for it brilliant ale, and the tavern itself smelled of brewer's yeast.

History
After the eruption, when the owners had been driven out or killed, ash zombies took residence in the tavern—some even gathered under the bar.

## Brughor Axe-Biter

Brughor Axe-Biter was the leader of a group of scouts from the Many-Arrows tribe in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Axe-Biter was a ferocious brute who led six other orcs in his scouting unit. However, Brughor himself was much more fond of raiding and killing, than he was for just scouting. His orcs would flee from combat if he were slain. Brughor was much more vigorous than ordinary orcs, and was able to survive far longer against blows in combat. As a result of pillaging, Axe-Biter had accumulated a decent amount of currency, as well as three bottles of perfume.

History
Circa 1491 DR, Brughor's group moved south, burning many houses on their way. They eventually moved to a cave in Wyvern Tor and found an ogre ally, called Gog. After hearing rumors of their existence, the townmaster of Phandalin, Harbin Wester, wished for someone to clear out Brughor and his band.

## Brown pudding

A brown pudding was an uncommon relative of the black pudding that was tougher and suited to marshy environments.

Description
Brown puddings were the most naturally armored of the black pudding's relatives, but their attacks were not as strong.

Like other puddings, they were non-intelligent, and wandered alone. They could grow up to  in diameter.

Ecology
Instead of corroding metals, brown puddings dissolved wood and leather. They consumed items made of such materials in about a minute, even if they were highly enchanted.
Habitats
They were known to be found among traps in Undermountain on Toril, and even in the Castle in the Tears of Selûne asteroid cluster. In this instance, the dangerous puddings fed upon ravens and grass. They hid themselves very well, making them notoriously difficult to eradicate.

In the Abyss, brown puddings could be found in Shedaklah, the Slime Pits, 222nd layer of the Abyss.

## Bracers of archery

Bracers of archery, also known as bracers of the perfect shot, were magical bracers that empowered their wearer with a supernatural skill with bows.

Description
Typical bracers of archery looked like normal leather protective wristbands.

Powers
A creature attuned to a set of bracers of archery became proficient with shortbows and longbows even without prior training. In addition, arrows shot by someone wearing bracers of archery were more penetrating and damaging than normal. This benefit only worked if both bracers were worn.

Notable Bracers of Archery
The spirit of the Chultan trickster god Kubazan inhabited a set of bracers of archery that was adorned with carved images of froghemoths. The skin of anyone wearing those bracers was kept disturbingly moist.

The Dale's Protector was a set of bracers that originated from the Great Dale.

## Briel's Book of Shadows

Briel's Book of Shadows was an untitled spellbook.

Description
The folio was bound with leather and contained 21 pages of stained and worn parchment.

History
Although untitled, it was commonly referred to as Briel's Book of Shadows. The folio was widely considered to have been penned by the archmage Briel while he was an apprentice under Aumvor. Ubergast, the sage, pointed to some evidence that supports this. He discovered the handwriting within the book was similar to a letter penned by Briel which was found in the archives of Baldur's Gate.

Contents
The book contained notes, musings, ramblings, and spells. It had a chapter on the uses of alicorns and the instructions to create a homunculus. It held the following spells:
affect normal fires • cantripThe 1 edition version of Briel's Book of Shadows was described in Dragon #97 and had the cantrips exterminate, smokepuff, tie, and wilt. These were replaced by the 2 edition cantrip spell in Pages from the Mages. • scatterspray (unique) • shield • write

## Breathblock

Breathblock was a defensive spell that could only be cast by dragons.

The spell was similar to the shield spell used by humanoid wizards. When cast, an invisible barrier of force manifested around the dragon or an area. The barrier was  on each side. The barrier was mobile, so it could be used by a dragon as a shield. If used against the breath weapon of another dragon, the weapon's effects are either deflected away or reflected back to the attacking dragon.

This spell required only a verbal component.

## Bright Nydra

Bright Nydra  was a demigod and later an aspect of Selûne.

Description
Nydra had two avatars, one was a pale-skinned human woman, wrapped from head to toe in a toga of her own silver hair. Said skin was tinged with blue highlights. Her eyes were noted to have a strange focus about them, as if concentrating on things that were no longer apparent. She is also noted as having a fey-like beauty.

Her other avatar was that of a shaft of bright moonlight run through with silver faerie fire.

Personality
Bright Nydra was noted as being a caring deity, but her emotions changed with the phases of the moon. When she was upset, she would put on a cheery and hopeful facade.

Divine Realm
Nydra had no divine realm, as she dwelled bodily upon Toril.

Activities
Nydra appeared to those who were depressed and/or lost. She would attempt to cheer them up and lead the lost home. When mortals had earned her favor, she placed tear-shaped diamonds for them to find.

Relationships
Bright Nydra was allied with Chauntea, Eldath, Lathander, and Mielikki - all of whom are part of the pantheon of deities led by Nydra who were worshiped by the Marsh Drovers.

She counted Auril as an enemy.

History
Nydra was said to have been the daughter of Selûne and Shaundakul, who fell to Faerûn from the Tears of Selûne, supposedly in the 4th century Dalereckoning on a midwinter night. She appeared as a human woman to refugees fleeing a goblinoid horde in the High Moors north of the Storm Horns, leading them west into the Farsea Marshes where they would became the Marsh Drovers.

At some point after this, Selûne absorbed her divine essence, making Nydra an aspect of herself rather than a separate entity.

During the Time of Troubles, after escaping his imprisonment in High Horn, Nydra is said to have defeated the avatar of Bhaal and driven him out of the Farsea Marshes while he was pursuing Midnight.

Branoch was a shopkeeper of the Bruenghor Clan of the Sunset Mountains.

Description
The dwarf wore a light-purple jerkin, blue shorts, runic bracers, and woolly boots. He had a braided black beard, a mustache, pale skin covered in tattoos, and mid-length black hair.

He sold several weapons, both magical and ordinary. He claimed that he had more than enough weapons for everyone. Branoch also sold a variety of armor, potions, and other goods.

## Brogan

Brogan was a Harper that lived in the Sunset Mountains.

Description
The dwarf wore woolly boots, a ragged brown-blue outfit, and a belt. Brogan had a brown mohawk and a beard.

History
In 1374 DR, Brogan, being the eyes in this region for the Harpers, saw something amiss. The gnolls and ogres began to band together, and the sighting a white dragon was reported. The dark elves of the region seemed to be working with the other creatures, which caused him to worry. After hearing the drow plans, Brogan realized that they wished to exterminate the dwarven settlement on the Sunset Mountains, in order to protect the other creatures. In return, the drow would be promised the riches of the mines. In fact, a teleportation gate from the area to the city of Baldur's Gate had been constructed, potentially meaning that someone wished to move the creatures to the city. Brogan noticed that the portal actually lead the the Marsh of Chelimber, and he believed there was a gate that lay within the nearby ice cave.

Brogan was later taken captive by a group or dark elves located in a mine close to where he was stationed. When rescued, he mentioned to his saviors that the leader of the drow, a priestess, made use of some sort of horn to enter the ice cave. He advised his rescuers to travel to the Marsh of Chelimber and find out what was really going on.

## Bruenghor Clan

The Bruenghor Clan was a clan of dwarves that dwelt in the Sunset Mountains.

History
As of the, Halgren Death's Head embarked on a quest to earn coin to fix his family's reputation. He was a strong warrior whose bloodline was seemingly tainted by his rumored duergar ancestor and a criminal named Kaervas. In the city of Luskan, Halgren assembled an adventuring mercenary company he named the Annals of Halgren in order to uplift the name of his clan with fame and treasures.

In 1374 DR, the dwarves of the Bruenghor Clan were driven out of their mines by drow, who were led by the priestess Ilivarra. The clan quickly collapsed the entrances of the mine and escaped to the surface. The leader of the clan, Kolgrim, assembled a force and gathered the equipment needed to light the signal tower atop Mount Burning Eye, to alert the dwarves of Easting. However, his group (included the member with the key to the mines) were assailed by the ice dragon, Ciraxis, and a legion of ogres. Many were brutally slain and frozen to death, but Kolgrim managed to escape to a nearby ice cave.

Soon after, a trio of adventurers (Vahn, Adrianna, and Kromlech Bruenghor who was also a member of the clan) arrived. They met with Kolgrim, who was beyond healing, and he told them to light the signal fire. The three adventurers were successful, and even acquired the key to the mines. Here, they slew Ilivarra, and the threat to the clan was ended.

Notable Members
 Alia the Shield-Maiden
 Halgren Death's Head
 Kaervas
 Kromlech Bruenghor

## Breath of bewilderment

Breath of bewilderment was an offensive wizard spell.

Effects
The caster of this spell could expel a cloud of smoking vapor from their mouth. The vapor covered an area measuring 10 feet high by 10 feet wide by 30 feet long. The cloud only remained for one minute before dissipating, but any living creature caught within the cloud was stunned and unable to function for the same period of time.

In addition to being immune to the effects of their own spell when cast, the caster was also unaffected by the spell when cast by anyone else if the spell was committed to memory.

Components
This spell only required verbal and somatic components to cast.

## Brightsong

Brightsong was a wizard spell from the schools of abjuration, alteration, and enchantment.

Effects
Brightsong was unleashed when the incantation was sung. This spell created a sphere of free action centered around the caster. The sphere would follow the caster if they moved about but did not work under water. In addition to the benefits of free action, all locks, including magical ones, were opened and all knots were untied within the sphere.

There was a chance that any creature under a form of magical compulsion, such as a geas, would be freed from the compulsion when entering the brightsong sphere.

Components
This spell only required a Verbal component to cast.

## Brain spider (spell)

Brain spider was a wizard spell from the schools of divination and enchantment.

Effects
This spell allowed the caster to read the thoughts of up to eight targets at once and allowed them to dig around for specific information. Casters with more expertise were able to gather more information. The caster could only focus on thoughts from one target at a time. Additionally they could impart suggestions, as per the spell, to a single target at a time.

Any targets with psionic abilities, or those casting enchantment spells when the brain spider entered their minds, were aware of the intrusion and knew the caster's general location, unbeknownst to the caster. Insane and feebleminded targets could not be affected by this spell and the contact actually harmed the brain spider caster.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as a material component any type of spider to cast. The spider could be dead or alive but needed to have all eight legs.

## Brost

Brost, also called the Mushroom Village, was a trade town in the northern part of the nation of Tethyr in the Lands of Intrigue. It was situated centrally along a trade route known as the Tethir Road.

Geography
Around 1370 DR, the town of Brost was an isolated trade town along the Tethir Road.  Brost was widely known as a stop for merchant caravans and travelers and was well known enough across Tethyr to earn itself the moniker of "the Mushroom Village" by those in southern Tethyr.

Brost benefited from its central location along several trade routes because the town was situated between the port city of Murann and Riatavin, as well as being the first town south of Esmeltaran along a trail that wound west around the mountain range known as the Small Teeth.

Though isolated from many other Tethyrian settlements of the area, Brost developed a close relationship with the elves of Wealdath. So, despite being somewhat isolated from the aid of their countrymen, the town of Brost was still able to maintain stability for itself and the surrounding area because of the alliance with the Duchy of Noromath and by proxy the elves in the Forest of Tethir.

History
The settlement began as a hunting lodge named after the first settler, Kehlad Brost. The location later became a way station for caravans along the trade route after which farms began to be established in the area. After that, it didn't take long for hospitality and wainwright services to begin doing business and a town was born. The production of local ales and ciders, as well as produce such as peaches and other crops along with trade in mushrooms from the surrounding area, for which the town became known, gave the local farmers something to be traded and moved along the Tethir Road.

## Breeches

Breeches, also known as britches and occasionally as pants, were a form of outergarment for humanoid races. They covered the hips and legs, all the way down to the knees.

Description
Breeches came in a wide variety of styles, colors, and materials. Those sold from Aurora's Emporium were known to have a draw-string waist, allowing the wearer ease in adjusting them, as well as double stitching for extra strength. They were often worn alongside some form of codpiece.

History
Though the exact date of their invention on Toril was unknown, breeches dated back to the days of the Netheril empire.In the adventure module How the Mighty Are Fallen, which is set within the Netheril empire, players encounter a frozen skeleton in the Eastern Forest that is described as wearing breeches. Netheril breeches had a style distinct from that of breeches in the Present Age.

In 1491 DR, members of the Cult of the Eternal Flame took up residence beneath a ruined temple of Cyric in Mulmaster. There they wore leather aprons and breeches as they worked at braziers to sacrifice people as part of a process to reanimate azer.

Varieties
 Cannon breeches A fashionable, tube-like form of breeches that fit tightly to the wearer's legs. They were usually embroidered in gold, silver, silver thread, or colored silks.

Reputation
Up until the late 15 century DR, breeches were generally considered a form of clothing reserved for men. By the late-15 century it was commonplace for both men and women across the Realms to wear breeches, often of the leather variety.

Some people believed that breeches were a sign of civilized society, at least according to Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue.

Notable Wearers of Breeches
General
 In Amphail's annual Rite of the Stag Lass celebration, it was tradition for the eponymous lass to dress in a pair of dark green breeches. This village also had a garment shop, Imbryl's Cloaks, that was known to sell breeches.
 Breeches made of leather were often worn by blacksmiths and dockworkers. They were also typically worn by women when gardening or traveling in a muddy environment.
Individuals
 Ardenor Eldritch
 Artus Cimber
 Baylee Arnvold, an archaeologist and ranger, wore deerskin breeches.
 Dhusarra el Abhuk
 Dove Falconhand
 Ffellsil, a Netherese wizard, was known to wear wrapped breeches.
 Giogioni Wyvernspur
 Jherek Wolf's-get
 Myschanta Halarra, the Arabellan abjurer, wore breeches as part of her unpretentious outfit.
 Regis
 Talatha Vaerovree
 Tessaril Winter
 Valas Hune
 Xale of the Starry Glen was known to wear tailored breeches cut in the ancient style of Netheril breeches.

Races & Intelligent Creatures
 Leather breeches were usually the only form of clothing that korreds were seen to wear.
 Kobolds were typically known to dress in breeches.
Regions & Settlements
 In Barovia, it was quite common for men to wear heavy breeches.
 The City Guard of Waterdeep typically wore leather breeches in a variety of hues, often mismatched, whenever they went undercover. Their "undress" uniform also featured breeches, but ankle-length and of a gray hue.
 Citizens of Mulmaster typically dressed in leather breeches.

Religions
 Priests of Deneir typically wore breeches as part of the priestly vestments.
 Newly accepted members in the Church of Bane's clergy were given black tunics and crimson breeches as their priestly vestments.
 Both clerics and druids of Silvanus typically wore green breeches as part of their priestly vestments.
 Breeches were among the types of garments the priests of Uthgar typically wore during high rituals at their ancestral mounds.

## Brain in a jar

A brain in a jar was the brain of a creature preserved and animated through alchemy and undeath. Liberated of its body, it was capable of great and disturbing psionic powers.

Creation
A masterwork of the art of preservation of biological matter, a brain in a jar was the product of both alchemy and necromancy, maintaining life in a state of undeath. It was not sufficient to pluck the brain of a zombie or other undead and put it in preservatives, as it would still lack thought and psionic power. Instead, creation of a true brain in a jar required a specific extraction ritual, spells of formulation, and recipes of alchemical preservation, all of which were well-kept secrets known to a select few master necromancers.

Whatever the process, it was difficult and hazardous for, as its powers grew, the brain in a jar was apt to dominate the mind of the necromancer foolish enough to create it.

Description
As the name said, it was a disembodied brain floating within a glass canister filled with alchemical preservative fluids (such as embalming fluid). The brain was shrunken and wrinkled in undeath, and the jar grew grimy with time. In all, brain, jar, and fluid typically weighed around .

Abilities
At will, a brain in a jar could launch a mind thrust, which inflicted an intense psychic assault on the mental pathways of a target creature, undermining its intellect and doing physical harm if they lacked the will to resist.

Thrice a day each, it could make a suggestion that would influence a victim's behavior and move objects and creatures with the power of telekinesis. It could also mentally dominate a person once a day. It could communicate via telepathy with any being capable of language in . Being undead, it could also command and rebuke other undead in the same manner as a cleric, but it was resistant to being turned itself.

Using any telepathic or psionic power to sense or read the thoughts or control the mind of a brain in a jar, or make any other kind of direct mental contact with it, would cause the one making the attempt to experience its madness and suffer mental damage.

Not immobile, the brain in a jar was capable of flight, as fast as a humanoid could walk, with good maneuverability.

Combat
Being rather fragile, brains in jars were understandably unwilling to get into battle and would try to fly to safety. They would rather control than combat creatures. But when they couldn't avoid a fight, they struck at attackers with their mind thrust power.

Personality
No longer obliged to control an entire body, the brain in a jar acquired great powers of the mind. Instead, it controlled the minds and bodies of other beings, particularly the living. But it was also tortured and insane, and often twisted toward evil.

History

After the star elves of Sildëyuir executed the corrupt wizard Mourel Duskwalker, his apprentice Kyjal Stardancer retrieved his head. Mourel had been experimenting with a certain method and Kyjal used it to extract his master's brain, which he placed in a jar of embalming fluid. Then, using another of Mourel's techniques, Kyjal animated the brain. For defense and mobility, Mourel had a compartment cut into the chest of a commanded girallon zombie that could carry his jar. Over a century as a brain in a jar, Mourel evolved into a greater kind of undead, but he, Kyjal, and their minions remained trapped in the citadel of Tir'in'tiral sealed away in the Night Realm.

Notable Brains in Jars
 Doryx, a former alchemist and one of the Lords of Khalusk.
 Mourel Duskwalker
 The four elders of the Inquest

Appendix
Background
While a common concept in science fiction and horror, the brain in a jar in D&D may be based on the Living Brain of Rudolph Von Aubrecker created by  Victor Mordenheim in the Ravenloft setting.

Appearances

References

Briosar Helmsing was a high priest ranger of Mielikki in the 14 century DR.

History
Between 1357 - 1367 DR Briosar operated a shrine to Mielikki in Waterdeep's Sea Ward that was known as The Lady's Hands. Around 1367 DR it joined together with the nearby Shrine of Silvanus to form a temple complex known as the Shrines of Nature, which he operated jointly with the other shrine's high priest, Anarakin Iriboar.The Shrines of Nature first appear in the City of Splendors (boxed set), which is set during 1367 DR. ## Brieme

Brieme was a noviere eladrin who served as an advisor to the triton king Mirentos. She kept her identity a secret, always appearing in triton form when in the company of others.

Possessions
Brieme wore a ring of ixitxachitl warding and a shell necklace of protection, the latter of which was a gift from king Mirentos. In battle, she wielded an eel trident.

History
Ahto, one of Brieme's patron gods, had been pursuing the triton deity Persana romantically for a while, to no success. In an effort to win the Guardian of the Deep over, Ahto sent Brieme to act as an advisor for Torilian triton king Mirentos, who had been engulfed in conflict with Iakhovas and the sahuagin. Posing as the sole survivor of a triton community ravaged by sahuagin, Brieme was quick to win over the triton king, and would go on to become an effective advisor, covertly using some of the magics inherent to novieres to aid the cause against Iakhovas.

Persana was displeased with Brieme's position, as the deity harbored feelings for king Mirentos. The Guardian of the Deep had not made Ahto aware of this, so as not to offend the greater god, but did send out agents to subvert the efforts of the noviere advisor.

## Brightswords

Brightswords was a holiday celebrated in Waterdeep on Eleint 21. It consisted of parades and demonstrations conducted by the city's main military organizations, as well as competitions and staged battles.

Activities
The City Watch, the City Guard, and the City Navy conducted parades, demonstrations, and staged battles throughout the day. In addition, contests of skill with wooden weapons were held between veteran soldiers and candidates interested in enlisting.

Boxing and wrestling tournaments between the Wards of Waterdeep were also held during Brightswords. Independent performers also held weapon juggling and throwing displays. During the entire holiday, weapon makers and merchants openly sold their products throughout the city.

At the end of the day, the most anticipated event was an aerial demonstration performed by the Griffon Cavalry. It was held at the Field of Triumph and consisted of staged battles against illusions of enemies provided by the Watchful Order of Magists and Protectors over the crowded stadium. Before the show, the surrounding areas were cleared of horses.

## Bramble

Brambles were a small race of evil fey.

Description
Brambles were a humanoid looking race that on average grew to be  tall. Their skin was dark and wrinkled, they had long pointed finger and toe nails, and their ears pointed to a degree beyond that of elves. A brace of spines stuck out from their backs, that looked as though they could support miniature dragon wings.

Many of the features of brambles were hidden beneath plate mail armor that they typically wore. This armor was covered in artificial spines and barbs that harmed those who touched them. It also had holes through which their wing-spines poked out of.

Personality
Brambles were considered to be among some of the vicious and aggressive races of fey. They were supremely confident when dealing with taller creatures, such as humans, who they considered to be "giants". They would think nothing of challenging a "giant" to a wrestling match and rarely honored any bets they made regarding them.

Biology
Some brambles were known to secrete a strong poison from their wing-spines that caused a painful burning sensation.

Abilities
Brambles were capable of casting the spell charm mount. One in ten brambles were also known to be capable of casting a special spell of anti-flying once per day. While they sometimes used the latter for the purpose of hunting, they especially liked to cast it on flying faeries like pixies and sprites so they could cruelly chase them through the woods.

Combat
Though brambles occasionally wielded weapons in the form of barbed spears that they used like javelins, they largely attempted to use hurl themselves at their opponents and harm them with their barbed armor. Whenever faced with an opponent that was  tall or smaller, they would try to wrestle them.

Rumors & Legends
Some rumors alleged that the bramble race began as outcasts of more "polite" faerie society, most likely descending from gorses.

Society
These vicious fey lived in nomadic bands and were more than willing to use their strong poison against dissenting members. Whenever a band grew too large it would split in half, heading off in opposite directions.

They sometimes gathered small amounts of easily transported treasure that they used to bribe other creatures into manufacturing the special armor they wore.

Brambles were known to ride upon small creatures such as almirajs, hedgehogs, and porcupines through use of their charm mount spell.
Diet
Brambles were an omnivorous species of fey.
Homelands
Brambles were typically found to live in grasslands, hills, and prairies in regions with a temperate or subarctic climate. When not traveling they camped within thorny bushes and other forms of natural protection, using their mounts as guards.
Languages
In addition to the language of fey, brambles had a slim understanding of the Elven language.
Relationships
Brambles were a favored monster of the deity Lolth and the Queen of Air and Darkness.

Usage
It was possible to drain the poison from a bramble's wing-spines, but the output was applicable for little more than an arrowhead or a dart. It would take over ten or more to coat something larger like a longsword. In addition, some collectors were willing to pay a lot of money for a set of their unique armor.

## Brewmaster's cudgel

Brewmaster's cudgel was the enchanted club passed from one brewmaster of Hanseath in Myth Drannor's Halls of Light to the next one.

Description
Brewmaster's cudgel did not appear special in any way. It was wooden, heavy, worn-out, and knotty.

Powers
The club's enchantment allowed the wielder of the weapon to deal triple damage when used against the undead.

History
Before the fall of Myth Drannor, the brewmaster's cudgel belonged to the last brewmaster of the city, Durlin. He died wielding the club against the invading Army of Darkness in the Dwarven Dungeons. The cudgel changed hands multiple times after the city's fall, being passed along from one creature to another that infested the ruins of the city.

## Brikklext

Brikklext was a hamlet located in the Upperdark under the southern foothills of the Earthspur Mountains, between Impiltur and the Vast.

Description
This hamlet, unlike other goblin communities, was somewhat civilized. The tribe was ruled by a mixed council of blues (psionic goblins) and bugbears, and together they managed to force the goblins out of their usual squalor and lack of perspective, guiding them into a more cohesive society.

History
The peculiarity of Brikklext was its underground water reservoir. Under the blues' direction, the goblins diverted a spring that once brought water to the surface. By digging and dam building, they redirected its waters to flood their own underground caves, creating a vast reservoir of clean drinking water.

When their spring dried up, the surface people had to accept trade with the goblins, who began appearing everyday with loads of water casks for sale.

The goblin "citizens" of Brikklext accepted civilization slowly. The idea of codified laws and a trade system was strange to them, though some understood the benefits.

Appendix

References

## Brain spider

Brain spiders were a rarely encountered species of spider-like creature with an unusual diet. There was no known evidence of these creatures living in the Realms.There were no known instances of a brain spider in a Realms sourcebook. However, they are listed in Demihuman Deities as one of many creatures that could appear in the Realms to act on behalf of Loth. For this reason, brain spiders are documented in this article.

Description
The abdomen of these creatures resembled a gray, wrinkly mass of brain matter. Despite its appearance, this abdomen was in fact chitinous. And much like a true arachnid, its exoskeleton was supported by eight hairy legs that allowed it climb up walls and ceilings with ease. On the back of their four forelimbs were sharp points and jagged backhooks. On their heads were four eyes and a pair of powerful mandibles.

On average their bodies, legs included, measured around  in diameter.

Personality
Brain spiders were a semi-intelligent, though rather crude and stupid species. Cowards at heart, they would flee whenever one or more of their pack was slain. However, they displayed remarkable cunning when it came to hunting.

Abilities
Much like a true arachnid, brain spiders could inject their victims with venom, though they could not spin webs. Their venom attacked the central nervous system of those affected, traveling through nervous paths all the way to the brain. Paralyzing and then slowly killing them. When the venom's work is finished, a victim's nerves would be liquified.

In addition, brain spiders possessed a number of psionic sciences and devotions, based around telepathy and psychometabolism, that they would utilize offensively. This included the following:
body equilibrium, contact, double pain, ego whip, graft weapon, intellect fortress, mind blank, and shadow form.

Whenever they used graft weapon, items would be permanently grafted to them until they mentally rejected it. All items would received a +1 bonus. And any wands grafted to their bodies would be hooked into their nervous system.

Combat
Brain spiders generally preferred to ambush their prey in groups. Dropping down from ceilings or other high ground or appearing from shadow form. When fighting creatures that lacked telepathy, they would coordinate with each other through that ability. They would focus their efforts on one or two victims, then flee and wait for their venom to do its work.

Besides their venomous mandibles, brain spiders would rear up and attack prey with their hooked forelimbs. They often used their psionic ability graft weapon to bond objects, magical weapons, or wands to these limbs.

Another one of their favorite tactics was to stand over some weak ledge, pit trap, or quicksand with their psionic ability body equilibrium and goad victims into trapping themselves. They also believed the ability double pain provided them particularly good sport.

Society
These creatures had a strict pack dominance hierarchy, would lower-ranked members being completely subservient to higher-ranked members. Their leaders were crude and brutish tyrants that were usually referred to by pompous titles, such as "King Venom" or "Master Thug."

They often told each other tales of their most delicious kills, gruesome stalkings, and clever prey.
Diet
Brain spiders fed upon the liquid nerves and brain matter of intelligent and psionic creatures. They were sometimes known to prey upon solitary drow and mindflayers.
Habitats
These creatures were only found underground, in areas with a subtropical climate.
Languages
Brain spiders possessed their own unique language that was based around hissing.
Relationships
Brain spiders were known to act as servants of the deity Lloth.

## Bristle spider

Bristle spiders were a species of monstrously sized spider, covered in needle-like bristles, that emerged in the Realms following the Spellplague.

Abilities
These spiders could spray their prey with an acidic poison and launch a barrage of their body's bristles. They could also detect and precisely locate vibrations in their surroundings.

Combat
When confronting prey a bristle spider would launch a barrage of their bristles, disorienting and temporarily blinding them, and would then move in to spray them with their acidic poison.

Ecology

Bristle spiders typically hunted either alone or in pairs and were attracted to the sight of battle.
Habitats
Bristle spiders were known to be found either in vast, overgrown jungles or underground.
Relationships
These creatures were prized by both drow and ettercaps as guardians and companions, though other intelligent races were known to train them as well. In particular, oni trained them to serve as guardians and mounts.

For the sake of attacking prey, wild bristle spiders would often ally themselves with drow or driders.

History
In the late 15 century DR, bristle spiders could be found beneath the ruins of Cragmire Keep, inside the Cragmire family's crypts, as well as in the Gnarlroot Caves beneath Sharandar. Around that time they could also be found in the pit fights conducted by the Gray Wolf tribe in Vellosk.

## Brotherhood of Light

The Brotherhood of Light was a group of Lathanderite champions who operated in the North during the 11 century DR.

Possessions
One of the order's paladins, Dahaerlin of the Burning Brand commissioned the clergy at the Spires of the Morning to craft the mighty rod of Lathander.

History
In the, the mountain village of Hespheira was magically transported away from the base of Mount Sar to the Plane of Shadow. The paladin Dahaerlin led the Brotherhood of Light to the the terrifying plane and traversed its shadowy terrain for four months, eventually reaching the Darklands.

Finally the Brotherhood came to battle the dracolich Umbralax, achieving victory and freeing over half the missing village's residents. They suffered great loss however, losing their brave champion Dahaerlin. It was believed that he sacrificed his own life to ensure the escape of his allies and the people they saved.

## Brightfinger

Brightfinger was a Netherese arcanist that was known for his development of light-based spells.

Personality
This variator was interested in spells that had more practical than destructive uses. He was astonished that the archwizards of Netheril didn't feel the same way.

History
Brightfinger was born in . Growing up with a priest, he sought to develop utility spells that were commonly used by clerics. In, Brightfinger developed the light spell. Five years later, he created the light-based spell that was known as Brightfinger's dancers, though it eventually became known as dancing lights. His peers commended his skill, and Brightfinger started to develop a version that would suddenly explode once someone approached it, though it is unknown if he ever completed its formulation.

Despite the fact that his peers were impressed by his magical skill, he never chose to become an archwizard. Instead, he settled in Canlespiere. Brightfinger would be hunted down by the Ashwind Assassins for three years straight, until he met his demise in .

Powers
Bracers of dexterity enhanced the wearer's dexterity. These items came in many different enchantment power levels.

History
Sages and historians believed the bracers of dexterity originally came from the far eastern land of Kara-Tur. They were created to enhance the martial artists' abilities, making them almost impossible to strike. These magical bracers were first mentioned in historical recordings of the visit of far-eastern travelers to the sword coast, even though though sages thought these records unreliable.

## Braakus Sinjen

Braakus Sinjen was the apprentice and the right hand to the Guild Master Georges of the Shadow Thieves' guild branch in Undermountain in the mid–14 century DR.

Personality
Braakus Sinjen was a devote Mask worshiper and a talented thief. He often utilized his charisma to distract and disarm his victims, allowing the reinforcements to ambush the unwitting targets.

History
In 1370 DR, the Shadow Thieves guild under the leadership of Guild Master Georges returned to Waterdeep. To maintain secrecy, they created a hideout and a stronghold beep in the mega-dungeon under the City of Splendors, Undermountain. There the guild grew and increased its numbers. Braakus Sinjen was the Guild Master's favorite pupil and was leading the guild's day-to-day activities, such as recruitment, morale, and fending off the goblin raids from the nearby tribe. Braakus conducted his duties from a hidden shrine of Mask, protected by an illusory wall at the heart of the stronghold. That shrine was used for joining ceremonies of the new recruits as they took the oaths and committed to the Mask's dogma.

Eventually, the guild's activities were discovered by the Lords of Waterdeep, and a lone hero was sent to investigate and dismantle the thieves' operations. When confronted with the adventurer, Braakus Sinjen attempted to distract the hero with his honeyed words, friendliness, and offer of joining, only to let his fellow Shadows spring a trap.

Brialer Erkowe was a former adventurer who found a new life as an innkeeper in the city of Myth Drannor. He was the owner and proprietor of the Owl Towers inn, found in the Myth Drannan district of Dlabraddath.

Brialer was trained as an elven dualist and held the title of alurakar. As such his training offered him great skill with the Faerûnian schools of magic, abjuration and alteration. 12

He was still an adept caster after his injury, though it took him longer to cast his spells.

He traveled as an adventurer for some time until his final endeavor brought that life to a sudden end. Brialer lost his left hand during his final venture, though the exact story of the wound was unknown.

## Brandy Quickstep

Brandy Quickstep was a halfling resident of Neverwinter during the late 15 century DR.

Relationships
Brandy had a number of connections in her home city, including Tarley Bluebell, who spent her time in Protector's Enclave, as well as a number of Neverwintan merchants.

History
After the Siege of Neverwinter in the, Brandy and her associate Tarley tried to create an enclave to house a number of their fellow halflings. Brandy traveled to the city's Blacklake District and hired adventurers to scout out three houses—Brimbleton Place, Thatcher Road, and Chadwick Stoop—believing them to for sale. They were in fact ruins and overwhelmed with the sludge that arose from the Blacklake itself.

After that unfortunate revelation, Brandy thought to make an effort to help some of her merchant friends. She turned to some adventurers, asking them to recover some of the residents' personal effects that had been stolen by members of the Nashers, the violent faction of the revolutionary Sons of Alagondar, in order for her to return them to their rightful owners.

## Broken one

Broken ones, also known as animal men, were a variety of humanoids whose bodies had been spliced with animals through means of scientific or magical experiments gone wrong, a powerful curse, and, on rare occasions, by the Dark Powers of the Domains of Dread.

Description
These creatures more or less looked human, but their bodies were warped and twisted. The non-human parts of their bodies were clearly visible to any who could see them. By their very nature, the features of a broken one's body varied by individual, however, they never displayed traits of intelligent nonhuman races. For example, those merged with a scorpion might have a chitinous shell or a poisonous stinger, while those merged with a rat might have a long whip-like tail and wiry whiskers.

Personality
The habits of broken ones were often dictated by whatever animal they were spliced with.

Abilities

Common traits among broken ones were stamina greater than that of a human and a regenerative ability. Some, known as greater broken ones, were physically superior and healed at an even faster rate.

Broken ones also had varied abilities that were derived from whatever animal they were spliced with, such as darkvision or keen hearing. The common variety of broken one generally only had one such ability, while greater broken ones exhibited anywhere from one to four unique abilities.

Combat
Besides using their animal traits to their advantage, broken ones were known to fight with actual weaponry.

History
In the 14 century DR, the mercenary Baerduin Thask was known to have transformed his prisoners of war into broken ones.

In 1367 DR, just after Midsummer Night, the Kolat Towers were broken into and several spell scrolls were stolen, including ones that were inscribed with Duhlark's Animerge. This lead to an insurgence of vagrants in Waterdeep being turned into broken ones, with them occasionally being found in the sewers below.

In the late 14 century DR, a group of adventurers from the city of Ravens Bluff encountered both common and greater broken ones in the domain of Nidala, that had all displayed the traits of a scorpion and had been created by some evil wizard that was imprisoned in the dungeon of the darklord's castle. The broken ones were trying to rob a wagon of animal entrails from a slaughterhouse, fighting a bunch of ravenkin that were guarding it, before the adventurers intervened.

Society
These creatures were typically reclusive, but sought out their own kind. Whenever a society of them was found, it was of a tribal nature and consisted of ten to sixty broken ones of the common variety. There was often a greater broken one in these societies who acted as their leader. These societies did manufacture their own items for the purpose of survival, but they would be of no interest to outsiders.

When possible, broken ones would seek out the ones responsible for their creation and try to destroy them.

Diet
The dietary nature of broken ones varied depending upon whatever animal they were spliced with. Some could be strict carnivores, while others were herbivores.

Homelands
Broken ones typically dwelled in secluded areas far from human societies, such as in dense woods or rocky wastes.

Languages
Broken ones typically spoke whatever languages they knew prior to their transformation, in some limited capacity. Due to their deformities, it was not uncommon for them to speak in a heavily accented or slurred manner. A small number of them were also capable of speaking with whatever animal they had been spliced with.

Relationships
Broken ones occasionally attacked human caravans or villages. This was typically out of self-defense, a need for supplies, or simply a desire for vengeance for some wrong. Sometimes evil sages or wizards would capture broken ones to study them.

Notable Broken Ones
 Bloirt Waelarn was transformed by an archmage into what could be considered a broken one.Prayers from the Faithful described Bloirt as having been transformed into something "akin to a mongrelman", rather than simply stating that he had been transformed into one, and the closest thing akin to mongrelfolk in 2 edition would be broken ones.

Rumors & Legends
According to rumors, some broken ones were the results of failed attempts at the spells polymorph, reincarnate, and resurrection.

Appendix
Notes

See Also
 Mongrelfolk, a very similar type of creature that could also be found in the Domains of Dread.

## Broken Crown Inn

The Broken Crown Inn was an inn found on the High Road north of Neverwinter on the Sword Coast North in the late 15 century DR.

Location
The inn was situated just off the High Road and not far inland from Raven Cliff Beach and the ruins of Cragmire Keep and Angelhead Mine.

Structure

The Broken Crown Inn was rather modest in size: it was two stories tall and did not boast many rooms, though parts of it were incomplete and under construction. The building was timber framed, with stone-brick walls on the ground floor and plastered walls on the upper floor, while the roof had wooden shingles. The ground floor was given over to a taproom, while the upper floor had a few rooms for guests.

In front of the inn was a well and behind it was a water wheel that emptied into a nearby pond, though the source of the water and its purpose and function are unknown.

Services
In addition to room and board, the inn was a decent enough place to find work. Bounty officers could be found staying at the inn, offering recompense for those who helped rid the area of bandits and other criminals.

History

Its first owner was a sympathizer of Luskan who believed the rulers of Neverwintan should be overthrown, hence the name "Broken Crown".

Some time in the 1470s DR, the inn was purchased and re-opened by Gorun Pricebreaker, a dwarf merchant who sought to profit from the re-emerging trade traffic that had sprung up along the High Road.

Circa the, the inn became a hub of activity for adventurers, Harpers, and others who were taking actions against the Blackdagger Bandits that had taken over the ruins of Cragmire Keep and preyed on caravans on the High Road.

Notable Inhabitants

 Gorun Pricebreaker, innkeeper and merchant.
 Cecil Proudfoot, a halfling who sought to improve the lives of his kin.
 Melkhor Goldseeker, a dwarven merchant.
 Taggot Ironshroud, a dwarven adventurer and relic hunter.
 Seven Toes and Lanric, Harper agents acting to ruin the Blackdaggers.
 Spycatcher Westband, a Neverwinter Guardsman sent to apprehend Blackdagger spies.
 An unnamed drow operative working against the Blackdaggers

Bressana Wildmaster was an elf of New Sharandar who lived in the fey settlement during the late 15 century of the Faerûnian calendar.

History
When Malabog the fomorian king threatened New Sharandar circa the, Bressana and her allies took swift action against the dark fey minions of his domain. When Malabog's redcaps attempted to induce a feral state in the region's portal hounds, Bressana led the efforts to regain the trust of the fey canines. She also worked with some adventurers from Neverwinter to heal dryad trees that had also been harmed by the malevolent redcaps.

## Bronzewood

Bronzewood was a type of hardwood tree that was found in the land of Chult.

Description
As a crafting material, bronzewood was said to be comparable to some metals in its durability and strength while only having a portion of the weight.

Habitats
 In Chult, along the banks of the River Soshenstar.

Varieties
Living bronzewood was unique variety of the hardwood that had been magically brought back to life. This was achieved by treating the finished wood with some tear of Ubtao, sap that was drawn from a unique tree found within the Tomb of the Nine Gods.

Usage
Bronzewood was favored for its exceptional hardness compared to other woods. Craftspeople often used it to fashion handles for tools such as hammers, scythes, machetes, pickaxes, and even arrows. When treated with resin made from lakh insects, bronzewood could be hardened ever further and used in the crafting of weapons like daggers, axes, and swords. In some cases, it was even fashioned into chests.

History
Although it was not native to the region, the elves of Iliyanbruen were known to use bronzewood in the creation of their musical instruments.

## Brokenstone Vale

Brokenstone Vale was an isolated valley in the Feywild.

Geography
This valley was a maze of heavily forested ravines and steep hills, with a variety of old castle and tower ruins. A rushing river winded across the valley's mark and had stone posts on the far side of it that marked Brokenstone's boundary. Underneath the valley was an entrance to the fomorian kingdom of Harrowhame.
Geographical Features
Within this valley the moon appeared to be much brighter than in other parts of the Feywild.
Flora & Fauna
Wolfsbane grew on the mountain slopes of this valley.

History
Centuries prior to the Spellplague, a werewolf lord forged a kingdom of lycanthropes in this valley and waged a fierce, brutal war with the neighboring eladrin realms. This area of the Feywild was conceded to lycanthropes and shapechangers in an ancient pact with a parliament of archfey known as the Court of Stars. Their pact ensured that no creature could set foot in this valley without the permission of the resident lycanthropes, and entering without it would make their life forfeit. Conversely, the pact also dictated that any lycanthrope that hunted beyond the valley would be hunted by an archfey known as the Maiden of the Moon.

As the centuries passed, many werebeast rulers would raise keeps or halls within castles or towers. These would go on to become ruins that littered the valley.

Over time the lycanthropes of the valley felt chafed by their ancient pact and began to hunt far beyond the valley. Some in the Feywild would be inspired by the Maiden of the Moon to take up arms, hunting down such lycanthropes. Some lycanthropes swore fealty to Bronnor, the king of Harrowhame, plotting to one day ambush the Maiden of the Moon and put an end to her, while others had a loose alliance with the fomorian queen Connomae.

Following the Spellplague, some of the lycanthropes that had formed an alliance with Connomae made their way to the island of Moray on the Prime Material world of Toril and became members of the Black Blood tribe.

Notable Locations
 Along the river boundaries of this valley were tiny hamlets, clusters of ragged huts, where civilized lycanthropes lived and did trade with outside merchants. Bringing supplies and trading with them rare woods. These hamlets were overseen by a werewolf lord name Viktor Mazan, who maintained the brutal peace necessary for them to exist. He did not allow travelers to remain long and tried to send them off as quickly as possible.
 The mountainsides of this valley were riddled with decrepit mines that wererats laired in.

Inhabitants

This valley was primarily inhabited by lycanthropes. Some gathered within clans or tribes, but most lived solitary lives and kept to themselves. Many barbarian berserkers could be found among these tribes. Most of the lycanthropes in this valley rarely bothered to disguise their true nature. Some lived within the ruined structures built for werebeast lords of yore. Some lycanthropes were druids that gathered high in the mountains near fey crossings, believing that the key to controlling their urges lied with primal spirits.

Occasionally outsiders came to the valley to live as hermits. Non-lycanthropes lived high in its mountain, hunting in the woods by day and farming the lower mountain slopes. They would paint a silver stripe across their home to signal that they have silver and the protection of the Maiden of the Moon. Some would also display wolfsbane around their home as a deterrent.

Some lycanthropes within the valley acted as assassins or spies of Harrowhame's king.

## Braxat

Braxats were a nasty, brutish species that enjoyed tormenting their prey. They were counted as scalykind by other Scaled Ones.

Description
Braxats were extremely muscular, even hulking,  vaguely lizard-like humanoids with features that were a bizarre mix of spiny beetle and rhinoceros. The top of the head and the back were lined with thick, horn-covered plates and there was a single horn that jutted up between the nostrils.

Personality
Braxats were violent and cruel people that enjoyed slowly torturing their prey to death before eating it. They feared no one and had no respect for law or authority. Unfortunately, they were also cunning creatures. They delighted in prolonging a kill and heightening fear by stalking their prey for long periods of time, using their telepathy to create false hopes, and crushing those hopes with ambushes.

Abilities
Braxats had multiple abilities that made them more dangerous than their brutish appearance suggested. They had a single breath weapon in either the form of a  cone of freezing cold or of a  cone of spraying acid. A braxat with an acid breath disliked using it against prey, as it dissolved and ruined the flesh it craved. They were immune to the damage of their breath weapon, as well as psychic damage.

Braxats also had a few spell-like abilities, being able to replicate the effects of blink at will and confusion and feeblemind once each day, with some individuals being able to replicate shield three times a day.  In addition to having a thick hide covering the body, they further toughened their natural armor by using psionics.

Much worse, however, was their natural psionic abilities. The most "benign" of these abilities was that they could speak telepathically to anyone within  of them. They could also unleash a  cone of mind-numbing mental energy in a single blast that could stun enemies. They were also known to use the mind thrust, mind blast, thought shield, and empty mind psionic abilities.

Combat
Braxats hunted in groups called warbands. They preferred to soften potential prey up with their psionic abilities before entering melee, reserving their breathe weapon for use against any particularly tough or stubborn enemies. They did not wear armor, instead relying on their tough hide, but did use greatclubs as weapons.

Society

Braxats had a tribal society, with each tribe having between 20 and 80 members. Each tribe was led by a single leader, who proved they were worthy of leading annually in a ritual combat against a single challenger who had earned the right of contest over the past year through successful raids.

Young braxats were taught to fight as soon as they could stand, and lethal combat between juvenles over food, lairs, and potential mates were not uncommon and even encouraged, since it meant that only the strongest and cleverest survived to reproduce. Eventually, a young braxat would prove themself worthy to join a warband, which then became like a second family to them. Agression between bandmates was strictly forbidden, and and killing a bandmate was punished with the immediate execution of both the offender and their family. This was because cooperation was deemed vital to a successful hunt. Switching between warbands occurred very, very rarely, typically only when most of a warband had been killed off.

It was not uncommon for a single warband of braxats to wipe out several humanoid settlements during a single year. Survivors of their raids would sometimes band together to hunt them down in vengeance, and experienced adventurers were known to hunt them for sport, but braxats were more than capable of protecting themselves with traps and ambushes, and most such hunts ended in victory for the braxats. Those that succeeded yielded braxat-horn trophies, which had no value in and of themselves but could have intricate sculptures carved upon them by artists.

Braxats typically spoke the Giant language, although more "civilized" ones made an effort to learn Common as well. Braxats were known to train as rangers, less commonly as barbarians or fighters, and very rarely as druids.

Ecology
Braxats were nocturnal and carnivorous creatures that preyed almost exclusively on intelligent humanoids. They lived in mountains and deserts in any climate. They were known to travel deep into civilized lands in search of plentiful and easy-to-catch prey.

## Brench

Brench were a species of blue-scaled fish that inhabited Lake Miir.

History
Back during the days of Netheril, the small-net fishermen of Asram's port city Miirsar went out on Lake Miir on a daily basis to fish for brench.

## Bryn Goldtooth

Bryn Goldtooth was a halfling shopkeeper of the town of Ghars in Cormyr in the mid–14 century DR.

Activities
Bryn ran a business buying, selling, and trading all kinds of goods. He didn't get his stock wholesale from merchants, but instead from other customers, so he had a highly eclectic range, from vegetables to stuffed leucrotta heads, old journals to linen tablecloths, gold candlesticks to a variety of cranial drills (used). These were mixed together in the narrow aisles of his dim and labyrinthine store. It was said one would find exactly what they were looking for, or nothing at all. Nevertheless, there were limits; Elizabeth Clawthorn once offered a dead cat for the tablecloths, but Bryn declined.

He gave good deals to fellow hin, even one-quarter halflings like Jasper, and usually with a wink and a "We halflings have to stick together, eh?" Meanwhile, it just made his day to get the best of a human in a trade. He wasn't a member of Cormyr's Merchants' Guild, as first he didn't do business with merchants and felt no kinship with them and second he would've hated to give human merchants money for nothing more than a worthless membership.

History

On Eleasias 28 in the, Bryn was riding his pony Bupkin after leaving the Swamp Rat tavern when he sighted the ghost of the bandit chieftain Fastred and raced back to town in fear.

A few weeks later, on Eleint 18, Jasper went to Bryn's store to ask him about the ghost sighting, after it was discovered to be Dovo in disguise. Bryn didn't have more to add, but did try to sell him 0.5 bushels (18 liters) of turnips he'd gotten in a trade that day; Jasper settled for a cheap old journal with a Camber Fosrick installment in it.

## Bright naga

Bright nagas were a relatively weak subspecies of naga.

Description
Bright nagas had reticulated, white-gold scales and bright eyes that burned with an almost hypnotic inner light. They were about  long and weighed about . When angry, their scales would darken to red.

Personality
Bright nagas were intelligent and manipulative creatures that used traps to protect their lairs and to convince creatures to leave them in peace.

Abilities
Bright nagas lacked the venomous fangs and innate magical talents of other nagas, instead only being able to mock-spellcast a single first-tier sorcerous spell at will. This spell was imprinted upon their mind and could not be changed; most bright nagas mock-casted magic missile, although others were known to mimic burning hands or color spray, among other spells. Bright nagas did have a talent for casting even when in melee combat, however.

Combat
If forced into combat, bright nagas attacked only with their single spell or bit enemies. However, they used their labyrinthine lairs to their advantage, appearing and retreating over the course of—for their enemies—frustrating battles.

Society
Bright nagas preferred to live in warm mountains. However, a bright naga was known to live in the first layer of Undermountain circa 1372 DR.

History
Due to their weaker abilities and lack of venom, bright nagas were said to be the result of genetic flaws or unfavorable breeding on the part of the "true nagas".

## Braum

Doctor Braum was a medical doctor of the town of Ghars in Cormyr in the mid–14 century DR.

Activities
Doctor Braum treated the people of Ghars; his skills were adequate, but not great. For a second opinion, people went to the retired War Wizard Benelaius behind Braum's back; Benelaius thought Braum to be a good man and didn't want to offend him by letting this be known. One difficult patient was Mayor Tobald. Braum found nothing wrong with him save his gout and advised him to eat less. Naturally, Tobald thought this absurd and claimed Braum "couldn't cure a nosebleed with a wagon load of cotton". He was even less impressed with the snake dung salve Braum prescribed.

Braum made house calls.

History

When the beheaded body of blacksmith's assistant Dovo was found dead on the morning of Eleint 17, the, Doctor Braum was among those summoned to the crime scene. Although shocked by the grisly sight, Braum maintained his professionalism and was the first to identify the broken glass as coming from a lantern. Braum examined the body and estimated the time of death as 10–12 hours prior, which tallied with Jasper's witness account, and tried to ascertain how the killing blow was made (but turned out to be mistaken).

Brooches of safety were enchanted pieces of jewelry from Waterdeep in the late 15 century DR.

A small intricately decorated brooch, a brooch of safety was shaped to resemble a shield and had a glowing purple gemstone affixed to its center.

Powers
These enchanted brooches gave the wearer a chance of becoming invisible as if under the effects of the sanctuary spell, allowing them to avoid being targeted by a foe for a short period of time.

Notable Owners
Halbenet Soladonel: a moon elf cleric of Corellon Larethian who worked with other warriors of Waterdeep, performing missions on behalf of Laeral Silverhand in the late 15 century DR.

## Brakkis Elspaar

Brakkis Elspaar was a member of the Stone-Cold Reavers, a group of reckless adventurers who made their living sometimes as sellswords and sometimes as brigands.

History
The group tried to steal the presumed treasure hoard of the young white dragon Cryovain from its lair, Icespire Hold. However, Cryovain hadn't amassed any treasure yet.

Description
Brakkis was out of shape and had lost the benefits of youth.

Personality
He took advantage of any opportunities presented to him, resorting to thievery when honest work was unavailable. He was a follower rather than a leader. This suited him because all he really wanted to think and talk about were dogs and what he would do when he retired.

## Brownfin

Brownfin was a species of freshwater fish found in streams and rivers of Cormyr, the King's Forest, and in the cold waters of Dathyl.

Ecology
Brownfins were carnivorous creatures like the majority of fish. Brownfins were bigger than minnows, which they consumed, while river eels were bigger than brownfins and consumed them in turn.

History
In 1348 DR, delicious roasted brownfin fish were a part of Florin Falconhand's and Narantha Crownsilver's morningfeast along with roasted rabbit and fresh cavanter buds.

## Bronze serpent

Bronze serpents were a type of large serpentine construct.

Description
These serpentine constructs were made of articulated bronze rings. Their eyes glowed a bluish-white and their powerful jaws dripped with electrical sparks. They typically measured  in length and weighed around .

Abilities
Their bodies were immune to harm from electrical attacks, immune to spells that affected the mind, and could see in areas of total darkness out to a range of . Furthermore, electrical attacks made on a bronze serpent would heal its body.

Combat
Bronze serpents typically concentrated initial attacks on those that appeared most vulnerable to their fangs. They attacked by grappling and constricting the bodies of their enemies, then biting with their electrical fangs.

Ecology
As constructs, they had little to no effect on their surrounding environment, and could be found in any land ecosystem.

Creation
Bronze serpents could be created from  of bronze by a caster of above-average skill who could cast arcane spells. The creation process also required use of the spells geas/quest, limited wish, polymorph any object, and shocking grasp.

History
These serpentine constructs were originally created in tropical lands to guard the temples of serpentine deities. Over the centuries the secrets of their creation spread, making it possible to see them almost anywhere in the Realms.

In 1374 DR, bronze serpents could be found among the many constructs that guarded the Arcturiadoom level of Undermountain.

## Braetha Sturnshon

Braetha Sturnshon was the eldest daughter of Hlargrim Sturnshon, who along with her eight sisters worked at the Dryad's Dreams, the only festhall in Mosstone, Tethyr in the mid-to-late-14 century DR.

Description
Like all the Sturnshon sisters, Braetha had long silky brown hair, skin the color of ivory, and large cheerful dark eyes. She wore the Dryad's uniform, a sewn leather gown resembling that of an actual dryad, a dress made out of green-dyed leather leaves.

History
Braetha, Kalaeria, Charethra, Hestroone, and her five other sisters all worked at the Dryad's Dreams with their father. The women were in charge of serving food and drinks to the customers and entertained them, giving rubdown to sore muscles of foresters and rangers at the end of a long workday. The women greeted the clients with cheery attitudes and affectionate body language. On occasion, they perform a limited assortment of medical procedures, such as setting dislocated joints or fingers in for the locals.

## Breadfruit

Breadfruit was a species of flowering tree that produced edible fruit, for the most part only available from Nelanther Islands and Calimshan.

Description
Exotic breadfruit was known for its doughy flesh, hidden under tough bumpy green skin. It tasted bland but was surprisingly rich on nutrients.

Availability
On the Nelanther Islands, also known as Pirate Isles of the Sea of Swords, breadfruit trees were plentiful, often providing castaways and survivors of shipwrecks washed ashore with life-saving source of food.

In Calimshan a crossbreed species of breadfruit and date existed and was called "palintrike".

Like many other exotic foodstuffs, breadfruits were imported and sold in Waterdeep by some of the more adventurous and knowledgeable vendors.

Usage
Breadfruit main use was food. The simplest way of preparing it was boiling shelled and sliced flesh and pummeling it into mash.

Brianne Dragonsbane was queen of the Kingdom of Bloodstone in the mid–15 century DR.

Brianne Dragonsbane was assassinated in the, and the civil unrest caused by this event distracted the Kingdom of Bloodstone from the mustering army of the Warlock Knights in Vaasa. Spysong believed that Yarin Frostmantle was involved in her death.

Shortly before her death, she had an illegitimate son named Kaggen Entreri. The pregnancy was kept a secret from all but her most trusted advisors.

## Brynn Osstra

Brynn Osstra was a farmer that lived in Nightstone with his family during the late 15 century DR.

Relationships
Brynn and his husband Thelbin farmed wheat within the safe confines of the walled village they called home. The couple lived in a cozy cottage with Brynn's mother, sister, and his nephew Broland.

History
Some time during the latter half of the 1480s DR, the Osstra family home was partially destroyed when cloud giants assailed Nightstone with stones from the sky. The village's residents fled for shelter in a nearby cave network but encountered a band of goblins led by Boss Hark. Many of the townsfolk were slain while others were taken as prisoners.

While Brynn, Thelbin, and Broland managed to survive the traumatic ordeal, Brynn's mother and sister were not so fortunate.
