## Brikhalna Ipprszhen

Brikhalna Ipprszhen, also known as Dutch, was an illithid gambler, businessman, restauranteur, and criminal enterpriser of the Underdark in the 14 century DR.

Description
Brikhalna always dressed according to high society fashion, and was only seen in formal robes or jackets from the finest tailors. He also trained his tentacles to hang in a manner evocative of an elegant mustache.

Personality
Brikhalna lacked the innate malevolence so common among mind flayers, and was instead a charming and sociable fellow who sought out the thrills and satisfactions that came from business ventures and gambling. He was particularly fond of his own ale, and was usually seen drinking it.

He especially disfavored physical weaponry, seeing such implements as a crude method far below his urbane and cultured status.

Abilities
Brikhalna had the typical abilities of a mind flayer, including a powerful mind blast. He also knew a number of arcane magic spells, such as suggestion, charm person, charm monster, ESP, levitate, astral projection, and plane shift.

Along the course of his life, he picked up and practiced the skills of brewing, gambling, and fine etiquette.

Possessions
Aside from his wardrobe of finely tailored formal-wear, Brikhalna had a prized stock of expensive Haba na'Kyubazs cigars, and regularly gave them out as gifts to his friends. His most important possession was a jug of alchemy that he kept in the brewing room of the Gehirn Cibus and used to make his prized UnderDark Ale.

Activities
Brikhalna enjoyed a variety of legal and illegal hobbies and enterprises. He gambled with high stakes, fixed drider races, and courted the wealthy and powerful. He operated the Gehirn Cibus restaurant and accompanying Think Easy, and was also responsible for brewing the famous house ale, UnderDark Ale.

Relationships
Brikhalna held himself above other illithids, and considered himself a distinctly better class than the thugs and gangsters he dealt with. Despite these feelings, he treated his criminal cohort with respect and decorum if only out of practical concerns for their wealth and power. He managed to befriend the eccentrically uncouth beholder Vikhrumn Coll, and had a genuine and close friendship with the drow matron mother and crime lord Felyndiira Shi'narrvha, though they always seemed to be loudly arguing when seen together in public.

His prominence in the criminal underworld of the Underdark also won him the righteous and unfavorable attention of Sir Elliot of Kness and the Forthright Band of Investigators.

History

As a young illithid, Brikhalna fell out of love with the traditional mind flayer hobby of conquering and enslaving all creation. The peculiar Brikhalna left his people and circulated among the wider Underdark, fulfilled his passion for entrepreneurship and gambling, and lost several fortunes in the process. Brikhalna finally hit on a winning move when he began fixing drider races instead of betting on them, and soon had a plenitude of wealth and favors owed from the Underdark's richest gamblers.

He eventually settled down and invested in part-ownership of the Gehirn Cibus restaurant, and expanded it with a secret "Think Easy" club for his various companions and associates. He fostered his club as a neutral meeting ground for all manner of business, and enforced this peace with a hidden group of drow assassins that took care of anyone who didn't get the message. His club hosted all manner of customers, including the fashionable Felyndiira Shi'narrvha, the unnerving musical assassin Gray'shivnarien Taliain, the hit man Vikhrumn Coll, and the fence Gnormahlenvaey Catoneriehn.

Appendix

## Brimir

Brimir was renowned frost giant brewer in Jotunheim, the land of giants on the plane of Ysgard, and was the operator of a notable beer-hall and "settlement" in Jotunheim known as Okalnir.

Description
Brimir was a rather old giant, with tanned skin that looked as weathered as stone. He had long and a beard that were both white in hue. He typically wore a golden medallion shaped like barrel around his neck.

Personality
He was a very generous man, more courteous than most of the giants in Jotunheim, and was exceptionally intelligent.

Abilities
As a cleric of the Giant pantheon, Brimir had a handful of spells at his disposal. These included charm person, command, dispel magic, endure heat, hold person, prayer, protection from good, silence, and tongues.

Additionally, due to the planar faction he was part of, Brimir could potentially make someone magically incapable of raising a hand against him for a time by being generous with them.

Possessions
Brimir owned a sword fit for a giant, as well as a feather from the wing of a deva that was held captive in the major Jotunheim city of Utgard. He kept the feather in his bedroom within a clay jar.

Activities
Okalnir was both Brimir's place of business and his home. He had his own bedroom in the hall, which was the largest of the hall's bedrooms at a width of  and a length of . Though some claimed he hibernated atop the kegs of beer in Okalnir's cellar during the winter season, when the hall had less business.

Relationships
Among the giants he considered close friends was Habrok Icecaster, one of the jarls of Utgard. When it came to the philosophical planar factions, Brimir was a member of the Ring-Givers and was one of its few notable giant members.

History
Brimir was born in Jotunheim to a giantess by the name of Elli.

In the, a group of adventurers from the city of Ravens Bluff on the Prime Material world of Toril journeyed to Jotunheim in search of the legendary Well of Mimir so they could learn how to destroy a powerful githyanki artifact known only as the Great Machine. While on their journey they potentially encountered Brimir and his hall, though sources differed on why this came about and how it went.

Some sources spoke of them coming to Okalnir as honored guests of Brimir's friend Habrok Icecaster, having proven their worth to him and his friends by hunting a bulette. He may have struck up a conversation with one or more of these adventurers about the Ring-Givers.

Later that night the Ravenians partook in Okalnir's nightly feast, where the thighs bones of the bulette slain earlier that day were cracked open and roasted. Before the feast began proper, Brimir stood and formally welcomed the party, extending his hospitality and speaking of their courage in the face of battle against the creature, garnering cheer from the assembly of giants. When dinner began to wind down Habrok would tell everyone of how he met the party and of how the bulette hunt began, garnering further applause from the crowd. Brimir then invited the adventurers to tell the story of the hunt themselves, casting tongues on them so that all assembled would be able to understand.

Another version of events spoke of the giants that were gathered in Okalnir feeling indifferent towards the guests. This drove Brimir to wager a contest of four tests for the adventurers - eating, drinking, running, and wrestling. He was even willing to wager three magic items if they would wager three comparably permanent or major charged items. The items he wagered included a Svartalfheim cloak, a horn of Valhalla, and a great war maul known as Grungir's hammer.

In eating the adventurers faced off against the fire giant Logi and had to reach the middle of a  trencher of stew. In drinking they faced no one, but had to drink alcohol from a massive horn as fast as they could. In running they faced the young giant Hugi and had to make it to the far end of the hall. Finally, in wrestling they faced Brimir's old mother, as the giants felt any other competitor would make things unfair.

Some claimed the adventurers managed to beat all four tests and for showing such prowess Brimir chose to give them an additional gift, creating the girdle of Brimir's strength.

Regardless of which of those versions of events occurred, the night in the hall ended with all retiring to bed. On the following day, Brimir treated the adventurers to a breakfast of porridge before sending them out in the direction of Mimir's Well.

A third version of events spoke of the adventurers sneaking into Brimir’s hall on behalf of Torheim, a proxy of Thor, to steal Brimir's deva feather. Torheim desired the feather so he could more easily track down the deva it came from, wanting to rescue them from their imprisonment in Utgard.

## Brasker

Brasker was a warrior in service to the Cult of the Dragon in the mid-14 century DR.

Personality
Brasker was more sanguine than Holvan about the task but still believed they should focus their energy on slaying other chasers of spellfire, the Zhentarim in particular, expecting reinforcements later on.

History
Holvan and Brasker were sent to the caravan led by Orthil Voldovan from Scornubel to Waterdeep in late, which the spellfire wielder Shandril Shessair had boarded. They were ordered to capture her, though neither liked their chances of success. During one of the ambushes the caravan faced, they accidentally blabbed about their allegiance within earshot of Shandril, who was hiding inside a wagon.

Brasker and Holvan slew Praulgar, revealing him as a Zhentarim, in an attempt to ingratiate themselves with Shandril. However, when Brasker introduced them by name, Shandril had already overheard them to be Cult members, and launched spellfire at their eyes, blinding and severely injuring them; Narm finished them off with a knife.

Rumors & Legends
Their allegiance was known to the Zhentarim scriers Hlael Toraunt and Korthauvar Hammantle, and to the Red Wizards in the service of Hulrivior.

## Brian (dwarf)

Brian was a dwarf adventurer who journeyed on the Sword Coast during the late 15 century DR.

Relationships
Brian was part of an adventuring party led by a man named Aradin. Their group also included Remira, Barth, and Liam.

History
As of the, Brian and his fellow adventurers were hired by the Baldurian wizard Lorroakan to recover an artifact known as the Nightsong. To accomplish their mission, they served as guides to the archdruid, Master Halsin of the Emerald Grove, and made their way out to the Shattered Sanctum temple. But the powerful druid had a different goal: investigating strange goings-on related to mind flayer tadpole infections in the region.

The group was caught by the cult of the Absolute during their excursion. Brian was cut to pieces by the goblins and turned into a spit roast for their feast celebrating a successful assault on Waukeen's Rest. One of the goblins, Tracker Klagga, took all his belongings, including a pair of blue pants and some poetry written by the dwarf serving as a map leading to the Nightsong.

## Bronor

Bronor was a malaugrym impersonating a merchant in the Sword Coast North during the mid–14 century DR.

Possessions
Bronor was using coins from old Athalantar. He owned property in Luskan.

Description
Bronor had a slender mustache with ends oiled to points. During his meeting with Mirt, he wore black Luskanite silks shot with irregular clouds of white, small stars.

History
As of late, Bronor had been impersonated by a malaugrym; Mirt claimed to have known that for six years. During a meeting with the moneylender, Bronor's coins were insulted by Mirt after a negotiation, and he then explained that he knew of the malaugrym impersonating him. He required, in exchange for keeping his secret, that Bronor keep his kin from seeking to kill, capture, or detain himself, Asper, and Shandril Shessair.

Brinna met her untimely end in the . She was killed while performing at a tavern in Triel, by a cultist of the Absolute. She was among the dozen individuals plucked from the Fugue Plane, chosen by the entity Withers to aid in his campaign against the Dead Three.

## Bronze golem

Bronze golems were humanoid shaped constructs constructed out of bronze that were similar to iron golems in some respects.

Description
On average these golems stood .

Abilities
These golems could only be harmed by weapons with a +3 or greater enchantment. Magical lightning could slow a bronze golem down, whilst magical fire could heal them. And because their bodies were constructed of bronze these golems were not subject to rust.

Much like iron golems, some bronze golems could breath noxious gas, whilst other bronze golems lacked this functionality.

History
In the month of Hammer, in the, a group of adventurers from Ravens Bluff found themselves transported to the plane of Carceri after defending the githzerai fortress of Tah'Darr from a tanar'ri incursion. At the urgings of the fallen paladin Elendil, the adventurers sought out Mount Othrys and Cronus for a way back to Toril. When they entered the crumbling palace they found Cronus to be deep into some kind of Cosmic Game with the marilith Cucathne, who was entertaining him with the game in hopes of negotiating the use of Othrys as a tanar'ri staging ground against Tah'Darr. Cronus was unwilling to answer any questions that the mortals posed, but offered to gate them home if they would fight as part of his forces in the game.

As part of this "game of the gods" the adventurers each had their bodies and minds temporarily merged with a powerful fiend somewhere in the multiverse, then were placed in a combat arena. There they did battle with bronze golems and a weak child of the hecatoncheires, unintentionally slighting Cronus as they were creatures of his own realm. After the adventurers earned their trip home they found that the battle had actually occurred in Ravens Bluff, discovering the Ministry of Art to be in ruins.

A year later in 1374 DR, bronze golems were one of many varieties of golems used as guardians inside the Onyx Tower.

## Brinnaq Torlath I

King Brinnaq Torlath I "the Shining" was an Amnian monarch of the Torlath dynasty who greatly expanded the realm.

Relationships
Brinnaq was the only son of King Rashturl. He had many sons including Esmel IV and Jhanos I, and his only daughter was Leluia.

History
King Brinnaq led Amn in a period of expansion and mercantilism, becoming known as the realm's first "merchant king." In the, Brinnaq negotiated the Tejarn Purchase—buying the lands north of the Sulduskoon River and south of the Tejarn Hills from the cash-strapped kingdom of Tethyr. These lands would later include Riatavin, Trailstone, and other settlements.

He died in 694 DR and was succeeded by his son Esmel IV, only for his son-in-law Arbas to set about killing every male Torlath in Amn, including Esmel IV and his own son Esmar. The sole survivor was Jhanos I, who returned from abroad and reclaimed the throne the following year.

## Brenth Stoutshield

High Marshal Brenth Stoutshield was a halfling crusader who came to found and lead the Citadel of the Banner Raised Anew in Amn in the 14 century DR.

Personality
Brenth was highly driven and devoted to his cause of building a realm for halflings to live in, free of interference by the taller folk.

Relationships
The High Marshal built a strong alliance with Major Tharundar Olehm of Hillfort Torbold, and they worked together to protect their interests.

History
Brenth was an officer of Arvoreen's Marchers, a group of militant halflings and gnomes in Tethyr founded during the Tethyrian Interregnum. While the Marchers were preoccupied with defending the Purple Marches during the widespread civil war, Brenth had the dream of founding an independent halfling realm in the Marches. This plan came to naught with the restoration of Tethyr's stability, and so in 1367 DR Brenth resigned his position and began casting about for other opportunities.

Brenth found a fortune of gems while raiding the ruins of Lost Xandar, and then began new project in the lands of former Meiritin. He built the Citadel of the Banner Raised Anew on ancient sacred ruins, and used the new temple of Arvoreen as a base for halfling solidarity. Starting with a small cadre of young priests and devout followers, Brenth attracted halflings parishioners from the nearby farmlands, gathered tithes, and began organizing militia patrols in the surrounding area. These worked in conjunction with the Council Army at Hillfort Torbold, and managed to cull the population of monsters in the region.

During the Sothillisian War in 1370 DR, Brenth put himself and most of his troops at the disposal of Major Olehm, leaving only a small reserve to defend the Citadel and surrounding area. This proactive stance put Brenth in the position of potentially winning great acclaim and influence, and potentially furthering his goals of an independent Meiritin.

## Brent Crivens

Brent Crivens was one of the inhabitants of Reithwin, the town that once venerated Selûne in the Western Heartlands, sometime before the late 14 century DR.

History
Brent Crivens perished in a tragic event sometime before the fall of Reithwin, before the, and before it was consumed by the Shadow-cursed Lands. The event took the lives of Brent and almost every member of the Crivens clan, including the family dog, outlived only by Errick Crivens, who was, in some way, the cause of the tragedy. Brent Crivens was buried in Reithwin's graveyard behind the House of Healing in a cluster surrounded by the graves of his family. His tombstone read: "In Memory of Brent Crivens, taken along with his family on the same grim night. May their deaths be avenged." The Crivens family's grave markers remained relatively undamaged and legible as of the .

In the, the city of Baldur's Gate was under a looming threat of the army of The Absolute and was flooded by refugees from the Western Heartlands fleeing from the cultists. During the time of crisis, crime was on the rise, and the criminal organization known as The Guild was getting bold and aggressive. Brigid's husband Cormac was attacked by the Guild's thugs, mugged, and beaten. Brigid was outraged by the crime wave and was prepared to give a stern talking to the Flaming Fist mercenaries of Rivington where the attack took place.

## Braethar

Braethar was a powerful Amnian mercantile family during the 11 century DR.

History
The Braethars helped end the venerable Torlath dynasty with the help of their archrivals, the Villax family. King Niskam the Doomed took the throne in the, following the assassination of his great-grandfather King Imnel IV, and he was quickly slain in turn by agents of the Braethars. King Rhor the Lackey was installed as the puppet of the Braethars, but he was killed in the, by agents of the Villaxes as they installed King Dornak the Puppet King. King Dornak was then quickly slain, and the chaos only ended with the rise of King Erdhinn Wavegold I.

## Breath of Winter

The Breath of Winter was a mysterious, icy creature that hunted across Damara as of the late 15 century DR. Its true nature was unknown and hotly debated among local Damarans.

Abilities
The monster was known to steal the heat from its prey, leaving only frozen husks to be discovered much later. Many believed that the monster's breath neutralized all heat.

It hunted both lone individuals and groups of creatures.

History
The monster appeared sometime after the melting of the Great Glacier began to reveal passages to the Frostfell, which was said to be infested with monsters previously unknown to Damara. By the, folk were discovering the remains of victims that appeared to have been slain by the monster many years prior.

Local tales that attempted to identify the monster ranged from a white dragon to undead—perhaps chillborn zombies emerging from nearby Vaalathrim—to a living blizzard to a giant carved from ice and clad in crystalline armor.

## Brevin

Brevin was a gnome who lived in Baldur's Gate during the late 15 century DR.

History
Brevin lived in Baldur's Gate and he was determined to become a member of Baldur's Gate's City Watch, but he was constantly confronted by prejudices regarding his race.

In the late 15 century, Brevin got wind of a "training camp" organized by Enver Gortash that supposedly could help him make his way into the Watch. Brevin enrolled in it, but he did not get what he was looking for. Instead, his brain was extracted for experiments or usage in the Steel Watch project.

In the, Brevin's brain was put into a brain jar named Closed Mind that could be found in the Necrotic Laboratory of the Mind Flayer Colony beneath Moonrise Towers.

## Briarbosk

Briarbosk was a sy-tel-quessir village in the Tangled Vale

History
Briarbosk was inhabited by the wild elven clan of the same name. The village became the first target of the green dragon, Venominhandar's, assault against elven communities of Cormanthor in, but the local elves survived.

The surviving elves of the clan met their end years later in an ill-fated attempt to avenge themselves on Venominhandar. The attacker's on Venom's Lair died all and sundry leaving Briarbosk abandoned.

A couple of decaying tree dwellings was all that was left of the village. It was rumored that Briarbosk's ruins were visited by the baelnorn from the Vale of Lost Voices. Some said the baelnorn came there to reminisce about their bygone days of life.

## Breathe earth

Breathe Earth was an alteration spell that allowed wizards to survive in harsh environments of complete earth and stone.

Effects
Breathe earth allowed its caster to avoid suffocation in mundane situations such as avalanches or cave-ins, as well as on planes such as the Elemental Plane of Earth or Quasi-Elemental Plane of Minerals. It also allowed one to safely breathe in areas of semisolid matter, such as on the Quasi-Elemental Plane of Salt.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, casting this spell required a material component in the form of small green feather.

The duration of this spell varied with the skill of the caster, with the lowest possible duration being around 6 to 9 hours. The effects of breathe earth could be shared by the caster with other creatures by touching them, though in doing so the spell's duration would become divided evenly among them.

History
Around the 14 century DR, a group of adventurers from Ravens Bluff traveling through the plane of Acheron received a spellbook from the duergar sage Gheldoin that contained a number of planar spells, including breathe earth.

Briarposts carried tales of being haunted by "clutching ghosts" obedient to a powerful spellcasting dweller. These creatures might have been cloakers or something more terrible.

## Bruiser staff

Bruiser staves were enchanted staves of powerful physical might.

Description
Bruiser staves were simple-looking wooden masterwork quarterstaves from  to  long. They were sometimes carved out of stout oak.

Powers
Bruiser staves possessed a +5 enchantment that effortlessly guided the wielder's weapon towards their targets and could damage even creatures who usually were unaffected by weapons of lesser enchantments. This magic, however, granted a limited boost to the staff's damage output. Despite that, the bruising enchantment wounded each successful strike's targets, causing bleeding. It also had a chance of stunning the targets.

History
In the, a bruiser staff belonged to the Archmage of the Underdark city of Rilauven, Malavon Despana, who operated from the caverns underneath the Spine of the World.

## Brightstar Sash

The Brightstar Sash was an adventuring band operating in Faerûn in the mid–14 century DR.

History
At some point, the Brightstar Sash recruited Caladnei, then a minor sorceress, while they passed through Turmish. Though at first she was put off somewhat by their lecherous ways, she was too tempted by gold and adventure to refuse. She was a part of the team during a successful adventure that netted them in excess of 6,000 gp. She also saw, and used, her first portal while a part of the company.

Also while Caladnei was a member, Bertro was slain by a wyvern and Rimardo and Vonda were killed while trying to rob a nobleman in Waterdeep, also bringing the wrath of that noble's guards on the rest of the Sash, running them out of the city. Caladnei briefly returned to Turmish upon learning of her mother's death, to see her father. The last one of the original group to die while she was a part of the group was Thloram Flambaertyn, who died of a fall in the Great Rift.

Caladnei and Marcon were the last two survivors of the group. During an adventure in which they tracked down a house heir for Sarde Telchaedrin in Halruaa, Caladnei received word from Meleghost Telchaedrin that her father was dying and was urged to leave Halruaa quickly. She cast a teleport spell, effectively dismissing herself from the party and leaving Marcon as the only person who was part of the group when she joined. He later found new blades to replace the old party members.

Members
 Caladnei, later Royal Mage of Cormyr
 Thloram Flambaertyn
 Vonda
 Bertro
 Marcon
 Rimardo

Location
Bruc and Pishe's was a small two-story building with its entrance facing the hidden entrance down into the Guild's Guildhall bar and hideout. By the late 15 century DR, the business' sign was old, broken, and rusted with "Bruc and Pishe's Emporium" written on it as well as its previous name, "Tina Turki's Good Time Bazaar."

At some point before the Absolute crisis of the, Bruc and Pishe's Emporium was abandoned and claimed by the Guild. ## Brightbuckler Street (Ravens Bluff)

Brightbuckler Street, or Red Sails Way, was a street that stretched north across the western length of Tlasbras, a neighborhood in the Southside District of Ravens Bluff.

Geography
This street stretched between the western ends of Hippocampus Street to the south and Zeldazar Street to the north.

Along the way between those two ends, the eastern side of Brightbuckler Street was a terminus to  Whisper Lane and was intersected by both Castles Way and Deepaxe Way.

## Bradlie Tagart

Bradlie Tagart was an inept Director of the Special Advisory Council Guard of Ravens Bluff in the mid–14 century DR.

Description
At the age of fifty-six, Director Tagart was a  tall human man who weighed . He had brown hair silvered with age and a wrinkled face.

Abilities
Bradlie was trained in handling daggers, short swords, clubs, short bows, and knives. In addition to weapon proficiency, Director Tagart was a skilled gambler, runner, and knew the Common tongue.

Possessions
Director Tagart always wore a set of ugly but enchanted +1 leathers.

Relationships
He indulged his son Noland Tagart throughout his life, making him near helpless as an adult forcing Bradlie to arrange for his son to get a job in the Special Advisory Council Guard, and even choosing clothes for him.

History
By the mid–14 century DR, Bradlie Tagart had already spent most of his life in government. He was a master of filling forms and buttering up the right high-ranking officials. Perhaps to no surprise, Director Tagart's career was not aimed at bettering the lives of the common folk and citizens of Ravens Bluff but instead at political power and personal status, earning him some enemies among fellow officials and bureaucrats. Historically, the man always came out on top thanks to luck and political favors; however, with age, Bradlie developed a sense of paranoia and a certain level of arrogance, accompanied by a slight case of being addled, prone to mood swings, and drastic actions.

## Brownie language

The Brownie language was the racial language of brownies, a type of fey.

Speakers
Beyond brownies themselves, Brownie was a common secondary language in Faerûn among forest gnomes, lightfoot halflings, rock gnomes, and tallfellow halflings. Beyond demihumans, Brownie was also commonly spoken by other fey creatures, such as dobies, grigs, killmoulis, quicklings, and stwingers. And elven cats who attached themselves to brownie families typically spoke Brownie to a rudimentary degree.

Brownie was also common as a secondary language among druids devoted to certain nature deities. Such as the druids of Chauntea, druids of Eldath, druids and druid/rangers of Mielikki, the druids of Silvanus, and the druids of Sheela Peryroyl. Other forms of specialty priests in the Realms for whom Brownie was a commonly known secondary language were the Eldathyn peacemen.

Notable Speakers
 Alrina Skyeyes, a human wizard who traveled as part of the crew of the Osprey.

## Branch of Giving

A Branch of Giving was an enchanted unwilting sapling usable by druids and rangers.

Powers
Nature's magic was channeled through a Branch of Giving, keeping it green and alive without having to be planted into the earth. A druid or a ranger could will the sapling to grow two flameberries or two iceberries once per day each. To no surprise, flameberries harnessed magical power of fire and iceberries – of cold. These berries could be fed to an animal companion to grant them special abilities temporarily. Berries of ice granted them powers and resistances of winter and berries of flame – powers and resistances of fiery nature.

History
In the, some of the Branches of Giving could be found within the endless halls of Undermountain – the mega-dungeon underneath the City of Splendor, Waterdeep.

## Brimstone speaker

Brimstone Speakers were divine casters that saw truespeak as a gift from their gods. They blended divine magic with truespeak in a way that allowed them to breathe flame, and to summon celestials, in their unending quest to purge all evil.

Abilities
A brimstone speaker's signature ability, the first one they uncovered, was an utterance such that it granted them a tongue of flames which scoured  straight ahead of them with fiery power. The next ability they mastered was the heavenly entreaty; while at first, it was lesser, fetching only the aid of a Bralani eladrin, with increasing mastery they gained the ability to summon word archons or ghaele eladrins, and finally an astral deva. Alongside their increasing mastery, they developed a harder-burning tongue of flames.

Their practice of the Power lagged somewhat, and one who pursued the path to the end would likely never achieve its strongest spells.

Relationships
Brimstone speakers were common in the churches of Lathander and more rarely Kossuth. Common starts as brimstone speakers came from a prospective speaker's superiors noticing they combined zealotry with scholarly patience, and being taught the truename of the tongue of flames; from there, they were expected to balance both pursuits themselves. They generally operated from small organizations within their churches, rarely being there organizations of brimstone speakers operating on their own.

Powers
The wearer of a set of bracers of artifice received a magical defense identical to that of a set of full plate armor but with no drawbacks. The bracers only weighed a fraction of plate mail's weight and imposed no restrictions on the wearer's gesticulations, making them perfect defensive items for mages. In addition to protection, bracers of artifice boosted the wearer's focus, allowing sorcerers to collect arcane energies more effectively and wizards to memorize an additional spell from the 8 circle of magic per day. Lastly, bracers of artifice granted the wearer minor resistance to magic.

## Brachina

Brachinas, also known as pleasure devils, were insidious baatezu, an enhanced form of erinyes who specialized in the art of seduction and corruption, particularly the servants of the gods.

In the infernal rankings of devils, brachinas were generally concluded to be of the same rank as cornugons.

Description
Even in their natural forms, brachinas typically resembled beautiful female humans, about  tall and weighing about  with pale skin, red hair, and black eyes. They still possessed the black-feathered wings of their previous erinyes forms however, resulting in an overall form akin to a dark angel.

Despite their own beauty, pleasure devils typically disguised themselves using polymorph to hide their fiendish features and better appeal to their targets. Yet even with their disguises and array of alluring features, it was still possible to sense something sinister behind their comely facade.

Personality
Like all devils, brachinas were beings of law and evil, plotting and conniving like all the others and quick to betray even their own kind if it meant getting ahead in the hierarchy of the Hells. They enjoyed their higher status, interestingly not because of what it afforded them within Hell, but because it gave them the relished chance to escape its oft-ugly sights. In their eyes the Material Plane was a ripe field, its mortal denizens weak-willed creatures able to be harvested with not but the corrupting promise of physical gratification, and they had no qualms about who they seduced into damnation.

Abilities
Brachinas had a number of powers that aided them in the manipulation of mortals. For one, they had a host of spell-like abilities they could use at will, including charm monster, clairvoyance, enthrall, morality undone, polymorph,and suggestion; some also had access to greater command once per day. Pleasure devils could communicate telepathically within  and always had the tongues spell on them. Their very touch was literally toxic, driving mortals mad and destroying their wills on contact. Furthermore, they could supernaturally beguile living creatures so thoroughly that they were entirely under their control. This last ability had a host of restrictions however, as it only worked for a few precious seconds, could be resisted with a mighty enough will, could only be used twice a day, and required them to be somewhat close to the target.

But brachinas were also dangerous apart from their more social abilities. They flew well, and twice as fast as they moved on land, and had the usual baatezu resistances to protect them. Furthermore, their repertoire of spell-like abilities also included greater teleport, produce flame and  vampiric touch at will, as well as  plane shift (but only on themselves), and, if not greater command, then trap the soul. Lastly, they had a good chance of summoning aid once per day if they so desired in the form of a falxugon, two kytons, or six lemures.

Combat
Pleasure devils excelled in turning enemies into allies, typically conducting combat by flying in the air just close enough to cast morality undone paired with a hasty charm monster before, hopefully, ordering their new puppet to turn on their comrades. This tactic continued until the enemy was outnumbered, at which point they started hurling fire at the holdouts, although they were also equipped with cold iron short swords for melee combat. Only in the direst of circumstances would they summon assistance.

Society
Brachinas were specially chosen devils endowed with greater beauty, power and station, and with it the duty of corrupting the virtuous beings of the Material Plane, their souls made all the sweeter for their fall from grace. They were architects of mortal failing who bound mortals to their service through pacts of desire; once a victim's resolve and dedication was undone and they were bent to their will, they could then be made to do the bidding of the brachina's master as well. They worked doubly hard in their duties, and only upon success would they return, for the punishment for failure was severe. But their many spell-like powers were bestowed upon them in aid of an even greater corruption: the downfall of godly servants. Thus, the primary targets of their nefarious work were clerics and paladins. Given a choice, brachinas worked alone.

Within infernal society, brachinas were peculiarly pulled in two directions. On one hand, a unique feature of the erinyes form they retained (the capacity to bear children) typically led their kind of baatezu to resist promotion into new forms in striking contrast to the vast majority of devils. On the other hand however, pit fiends and archdevils used pleasure devils as playthings, discarding them as quickly as they selected them. As a result, brachinas were quick to take any assignment to avoid the brutal attentions of their own masters, and grasped at any chance to rise in status, ironically hoping to one day join the ranks of the very pit fiends who mistreated them.

Pleasure devils were generally quite wealthy in relation to their actual strength, though they kept this wealth in multiple forms. Their sense of self-preservation drove them to invest some of it in protective items and weapons, but all kept some coinage (about 10-100 gold pieces) and a few pieces of cheap jewelry (no more than 250 gold pieces). They also carried with them a gem of appropriate value to cast trap the soul on their victims whenever they were out on a mission.

Pleasure devils were particularly associated with sensual Fierna and Belial of Phlegethos. Their specific legion, the Walkers in Fire, were composed of squadrons of eryines and brachina wizards who soared screaming through the air dropping fireballs on everyone below, knowing their fellow devils would be unaffected. Clerics of Fierna were known to be able to summon pleasure devils using the planar ally spell, while aspects of Belial were known to lead entourages containing more than one pleasure devil, as they always needed some lustful devil or mortal nearby to satiate their rapacious desires. Aside from them, aspects of Glasya frequently associated with pleasure devils, while those of Baalzebul never had them around.

Ecology
Though native to the Nine Hells, pleasure devils spent most of their time on the Material Plane, using their plane-shifting to get there. Within hell, they were only known to predominate in Nessus, where the greatest proportion of greater devils resided.

As previously mentioned, pleasure devils and their erinyes inferiors were unique among the devil types in their ability to become pregnant (although it was unclear if this ability was gained from before or after their descent from the Upper planes). The rank of pleasure devil could only be reached from the position of erinyes.

History
Some brachina rose from the erinyes that first fell from their angelic status with Asmodeus eons ago.

Notable Brachina
 Lady Madori, a brachina in service to Mephistopheles tasked with keeping track of the developments of one of his hellfire cults.

## Brass golem

Brass golems, also referred to as brass minotaurs, were a type of golem constructed from brass that were typically forged to resemble a minotaur. All were constructed to fulfill a singular goal, set at the time of their creation. This was typically to act as a guardian, but sometimes to carry out vengeance. If that goal became somehow unattainable they would lose their enchantment entirely and be rendered as nothing more than a statue.

Behavior
Brass golems typically stood in silent vigil, acting as though they were mere statues, only coming to life upon a triggering event, such as desecration of a shrine they were created to protect. Once active they were relentlessly focused upon the perpetrator of that triggering event, though would still fight to defend themselves from any other attackers.

Abilities
Some of these golems had the ability to cast maze once per day on a single target. They were immune to the effects of their own ability, as well as any similar spell. They were incredibly skilled trackers, especially when inside their maze, though only when it came to foes designated by a creator or connected to their singular goal. And they could freely enter the maze it created at any time to pursue their victim, completely vanishing from wherever they were standing.

When standing within this maze, the golem would slowly recover from any damage that had been dealt to it. Once their victim was slain, the extra-dimensional maze space would cease to exist, except if the minotaur themselves was still standing within it. Upon the next use of their maze ability, a new extra-dimensional space would be created.

Additionally, they could only be harmed by weapons with a +3 or greater enchantment. Any electricity-based effect would cause a brass golem to be rendered sluggish in movement, as if effected by the slow spell. They could partially be healed by fire-based effects, though were not impervious to harm from them. And if find the path were to be cast upon the area where a minotaur disappeared as it entered its own extra-dimensional maze, the spell would cause both the minotaur and their trapped victim to be returned.

Combat
Brass golems were outfitted with either a +3 battle axe of wounding or a +3 greataxe of wounding, both similar in effect to the sword of the same name, and were their primary means of fighting. For some brass golems, the haft end of these axes was inset with a large, flawless gem, big enough that it couldn't be easily hidden within the confines of a fist. This gem acted as a focus for an effect mimicking the spell trap the soul, which could be used once a day. Where it differed in effect from trap the soul was that those targeted would be sent to an extra-dimensional space, not unlike that created by a maze spell. These axes typically shattered upon the destruction of their brass golem.

Ecology
Construction
The process of creating them took around five months, in a specially prepared lab or workroom, and cost upwards of a 100,000 to 200,000 gold pieces. About 1,500 of those gold pieces were spent on the  of brass needed to construct the body and 32,320 of those gold pieces would be spent on the +3 axe of wounding.

The  of brass had to be forged from its component ores, copper and zinc. When in a molten state these ores had to have powdered diamond sprinkled into them. About three handfuls of powdered diamond were needed. The molten brass was then to be poured into a mold, holding that of a perfect minotaur skeleton, with the brass taking the place of flesh.The 3-edition version of this creature omits any mention of a skeleton in its creation instructions. Thus it is unclear if creating brass golems resembling something other than a minotaur might also require skeletons of the desired shape. This part of the creation process could be done by either the creator themselves or by hired hands.

After the golem's physical form was finished, the creator must cast a series of spells upon it. The required spells included geas/quest, maze, polymorph any object, and wish or limited wish. Either wish spell was meant to be cast last, acting as a binding agent for everything else. Additionally, some recipes called for also using the spell Mordenkainen's sword, while others called for the spell strength. If it was desired for the axe of wounding to be imbued with the maze effect instead of the golem itself, the caster would have to cast both maze and trap the soul on its gemstone.

History
The very first of the brass golems in the multiverse was said to have been created on another world by a mage called Relnar the Just, to avenge the death of his priestess wife after an attack and pillaging of her temple by three barbarians. According to some sources, this was a temple dedicated to Athena. Relnar planned to use a wish spell to send it back in time to the moment of the attack, but the next night, before he was even finished casting spells upon it, he received reports that the brass golem was standing guard over the temple site as it was being rebuilt.

Around 1374 DR, brass golems were one of many varieties of golems used as guardians inside the Onyx Tower.

## Brain coral

Brain coral was a rare sentient and psionic species of coral that was so named for their complexly ridged forms resembling a brain.

Description
This coral on average measured  in diameter and had colorations ranging from brown, olive, and yellow. If they were ever removed from water, the coloration of brain coral would change to that of a boney white. This part of the coral was its exoskeleton, made purely of calcium carbonate, built up over many years by an animal polyp.

Personality
These creatures possessed an exceptional degree of intelligence.

Abilities
A colony of brain coral possessed a wide arrange of psionic abilities at a ninth degree of mastery. These included the psionic attack modes ego whip and id insinuation, as well as psionic defense modes mind blank and thought shield. Colonies also possessed two or more of the following minor psionic disciplines: animal telepathy, cell adjustment, contact, domination, ESP, and invisibility. One fourth of colonies exhibited the psychokinesis science telekinesis. Those who did additionally exhibited the powers ballistic attack and clairvoyance.

Those that possessed ESP would often attempt read the minds of visitors.

Additionally, brain coral had nematocyts (stinging cells) throughout their exoskeleton that would inject a paralyzing neurotoxin upon any creature that tried to attack them.

Society
Homelands
Like most coral, brain was typically found within tropical waters. It could be found within warm tidal zones at depths of a  or more, usually among the growth or a coral reef or atoll. They were particularly known to be found in the Sea of Fallen Stars.
Usages
A piece of brain coral was needed as a material component for the spell awaken intelligence.  A largely intact exoskeleton could also be fashioned into a sort of crystal ball.

## Bracers of brachiation

Bracers of brachiation were pairs of rather ordinary looking bracers that carried an enchantment making anyone who wore them proficient in the art of brachiation, a form of arboreal movement common many types of apes.

Powers
While wearing bracers of brachiation an individual gained an ape-like proficiency in climbing and swinging from vines, trees, poles, ropes, and other forms dangling, flexible objects. This allowed an individual to swiftly travel from place to place within dense jungle environments. Additionally, these bracers could enhance a wearer's ability to jump and leap, similar to boots of striding and springing, though this effect would only activate if a jump would culminate in some sort of activity related to brachiation. These included such things as moving through treetops, climbing a tree or pole, and grasping a rope, vine, or other flexible object vine.

History
In the land of Zakhara, the region known as the Cities of the Ancients was host to a variety of magic items whose creation were inspired by the gods that were once worshiped there in ancient times. Among these many magical items were bracers of brachiation.

Notable Owners
 Some of the couriers that worked for Spider's Fleet o' Feet owned bracers of brachiation that they had either purchased or stolen to aid in their job.
 Elwin McAllister, a sea captain in the Vast.
 Narlgathra, a red dragon, had a pair of bracers of brachiation as part of his treasure horde in the ruins of Myth Drannor.
 Selim, a tribal leader possessed a pair of these bracers.

## Broadbelt

The broadbelt or broad belt was a kind of clothing accessory that overlapped with belts and girdles in a way that combined utility with fashion.

Description
Broadbelts were large and heavy belts worn about the waist, and covered much of the midsection thanks to the inherent broadness. A solid broadbelt was leather with metal reinforcement, and hooks, studs, spikes, and holes that added protection and attachment points for other accessories, tools, and weapons. More decorative belts functioned as jewelry first and could be decorated with precious metals like silver, gold, and platinum.

When combined with an armored loin guard and bustier, the full outfit functioned as a skin-baring and highly ornamental piece of armor that provided at least a little protection, as worn by the adventurer Kendra.

When sufficiently tightened, the belt compressed the innards and functioned as a lifting belt, protecting the wearer from injury while lifting great weights.

Availability
Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue offered an ornamented broadbelt for 6 gp, dyed black or brown. The belt was sourced from Boom's Garden and was able to be combined with their loin guard and bustier, and had a twistable buckle that tightened the belt.

## Branding iron

A branding iron, or brand, was a device used to scorch an identifying mark into a material or the skin of a living creature.

Description
A brand was basically a long metal rod designed to be repeatedly heated and cooled with use. If intended to impart a specific design such as a symbol or letter of script, the head of it would have an elaborate shape that was pressed into the surface material.

Availability

Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue offered branding irons that marked each and every character of the Dethek, Thorass, and Espruar scripts for 8 gp, as well as custom-built sigils for 13 gp.

Usage
The most common use of branding was the marking of livestock and other animals with their owner's mark, making it harder to rustle and sell on the stolen animals thanks to the original owner's identity being plain.

Groups such as the Ashmadai and the cult of the Absolute branded their initiates with their god's symbol to ensure their loyalty, potentially marking the soul itself with their master's sigil.

Brands were also used as a means of indelibly marking a prisoner, criminal, or slave, usually in a highly visible part of the body. Branding irons could also be used as tools of torture, by the simple expedient of burning the victim's flesh.

A brand could be used as a bludgeoning weapon in a pinch, as it was still ultimately a large and solid piece of metalwork. If the brand was glowing hot at the time, the damage inflicted was much increased.

Magic
 A symbol could be created on a surface by the arcane mark spell, which was painless and temporary.
 A painful mark could be created by the brand spell, which burned a short set of script into a living or nonliving surface, and could not be removed or healed by most means.

## Bracers of defenselessness

Bracers of defenselessness were cursed bracers that impeded the wearer's defenses.

Description
They resembled bracers of defense.

Powers
While they normally operated identically to bracers of defense, in response to a dangerous enemy, these bracers changed function from bolstering the user's defense to completely thwarting it. Once donned, these bracers could only be removed through the use of a remove curse spell or similar. By the, they were known to be tremendous hindrances, but no longer reported to thwart completely all of the user's defenses.

Versions described after the Spellplague were instead considered a specific property that was held in addition to whatever other capabilities a given device bore. In response to sufficient damage, the bracers' curse manifested for as long as combat continued, adding a notable hindrance to the user's defense and protection.

History
Per sages, the curse creating bracers of powerlessness came from a mispronounced word while the rite used to create other protective items was being devised; the faulty ceremony, with its mispronounced word, was still used by some, leading to the creation of these items down the centuries.

One of the wedding gifts from Tan Chin to Princess Tsenya Bhrokiti in the  was a pair of bracers of defenselessness.

There was likely at least one set in a watery chamber of Undermountain, loaded with dead and well-decomposed bodies, in the 1360s DR.

Notable Owners
There were a pair of such bracers in the hoard of the dragon turtle Perizade.

## Brine dragon

A brine dragon was a fully aquatic creature more akin to sea serpents than any other type of dragon.

Description
Brine dragons were fully adapted to the deep seas, and could neither fly, walk on land, nor breathe air.
In fact, they only broke the surface of the waters if the proper incentive was given, such as hunger combined with a boat full of humans, or they were forced to, as they did under the influence of the likes of Iakhovas.

They were massive creatures with flippers where other dragons would have claws. Their heads were triangular and wedge shaped but still quite reminiscent of normal dragons. Their oversized teeth gave an impression of them always smiling, though quite an unfriendly one at that. The hides were rough, mottled, and craggy, with the scales irregular and not fitting quite well together. Huge discolored pieces of salt were pressed into the hide that could not be dissolved. Newborn brine dragons were but  long, while the oldest largest specimens could reach  in size.

Personality
Brine dragons were a violent and unpredictable lot, and almost all had nihilistic tendencies, even going so far as to eat their own eggs or young if they were hungry enough. They all loved salt to an almost obsessive degree and it was from it that they got the name 'brine dragon'. They would naturally absorb it while swimming the oceans, but if they could locate deposits, like in coastal salt marshes, they would eat it. They hated black dragons with a passion. They would attack with little provocation if they felt like it, and ignored soft targets if they didn't, being extremely unpredictable creatures, eating everything they caught and overpowered, including marine undead.

Combat
Brine dragons were very good swimmers, able to move through waters without causing so much as a ripple. As such, they were quite capable ambush predators while they themselves were aware of every disturbance in the currents around them.

Their bite caused terrible damage due to their great teeth, which would gouge large marine creatures such as whales or other amphibious types of dragons.

The breath weapon of a brine dragon was a liquid spray of salt and an alkaline substance that burned like acid, working equally well whether above or below the water, even against aerial targets who would be caught in a caustic rain up to  high. The brine dragons themselves were immune to acids, salines, and all poisons.

Society
The nihilistic egos of the brine dragons meant that they never had leaders nor formed any real society, being persistent solitary creatures unless mating.

They made their lairs by using their caustic breath and spittle to glue together coral and rock formations into convenient caves. With these caves as their fixed point, they staked out their individual territories, which could range from  to  across, and could change daily as the aquatic situation changed.

Once a brine dragon had laid its eggs, it was a matter of chance whether one or both parents stayed with the eggs or let them fend for themselves, or just ate them. This extremely random parenting was one of the main reasons for the low numbers of brine dragons in the great seas.

History
In the, early in the Twelfth Serôs War, the mighty wereshark Iakhovas compelled a brine dragon to attack Waterdeep alongside a force of sahuagin and other usually solitary aquatic creatures such as a dragon turtle.

## Branwyn Moonsinger

Branwyn Moonsinger was a Great Druid of the Moonshae Isles during the 15 century DR.

Activities
As Great Druid, she worked tirelessly to prepare for what she believed to be the inevitable return of Kazgaroth. She made her home in a cottage within the fey realm of Sarifal on the isle of Gwynneth, where she kept watch over the Moonwell in the Sacred Grove of the Earthmother on the eastern shore of the Myrloch.

By the late 15 century DR, adventurers were known to seek out her assistance.

Relationships
She was the granddaughter of the late High Queen Alicia Kendrick of the Moonshaes.

Despite residing within Sarifal, she had little contact with the fey of that realm. When she did, it was during rare visits when curious fey brought her gifts in hopes of asking her questions about Gwynneth from before their arrival in the .

History
Born in the, Branwyn Moonsinger assumed the mantle of Great Druid in the . In the decades that followed, she focused on preparing for Kazgoroth's return. After she declined to intervene when fighting erupted between the humans and fey of Gwynneth, the humans of the Moonshaes assumed she had been killed, leading them to anoint a new Great Druid in her stead. She continued her vigil, but by —at age 71—her years had caught up with her as she had taken no steps to extend her lifespan.

## Broom of flying

The broom of flying was an enchanted broom that allowed its owner to fly.

Powers
The broom of flying could fly for up to 9 hours every day. Some brooms could fly at a speed of, carrying a load of  without slowing down, but with any more weight it would slow at a rate of  for every  added. Other brooms were able to fly at a faster rate of  or even, if the weight was under  and would slow down to  when above that weight and were able to move at this speed up until  when it could no longer fly at all. All brooms could climb or descend at a rather steep rate of 30 degrees.

Every broom had a command word. If the command word was spoken and a known location, including layout, was used it would fly to that place. Otherwise, it would fly to its owner if they were up to  from the broom. A broom that could not be sent to other locations could instead be called from as far away as  instead.

History
In the Mornhaven Towers deep in the Mere of Dead Men a broom of flying lay hidden within a linen closet, with its command word etched into the handle.

Creation
To create a broom of flying, a grand sum of 17,000 gp of ingredients was needed, this often including roc feathers. The maker would need to able to create wondrous items and wield magic of some sort, as the spells overland flight and permanency were used in the creation.

Notable Owner
 Diancastra, the giant demigoddess owned and used a, presumably larger and more sturdy, broom of flying.
 Morda, a witch haunting the village of Woody Glen in the High Forest, used a broom of flying for scouting, combat, and to escape if battle turned against her.

At one point, the thuggish duo came across Abdel Adrian and his traveling companions in front of the Copper Coronet in the slums of Athkatla. ## Brelma

Brelma was a drow slaver, part of the infiltration of Scornubel in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Brelma was a drow. She employed magical disguises to take on other personas, but in her own skin, she was shorter than Qilué Veladorn, with a lock of smoke-hued hair above her right temple.

Activities & Relationships
She took her orders from Daerdatha, and acted as liaison to Auvrurn Labraster in Waterdeep through Mrilla Malsander.

History
In, Brelma was among the guests at a party in Halonder Eldeglut's home, where she was part of a crowd of drow planning to replace him and his wife Iyrevven Eldeglut with impersonators; they used spell-disguises to enter the party as invited guests. She'd been part of the infiltration party in Scornubel long enough to learn that the people replaced were all too often sent to Chult as slaves. When the drow at the party discovered Qilué Veladorn, a Chosen of Mystra, had replaced one of their own spell-changed drow infiltrators. After she was finally subdued, Brelma followed the sorceress Daerdatha's instructions to make a group mind-link and so overwhelm the infiltrator, allowing them to mind-touch her as a team. Brelma made her way to Waterdeep across the Sword Coast in the same night, presumably by teleporting, departing well after Qilué Veladorn's body was dumped into the Chionthar. She was inadvertently marked with a tracing spell when the woman bit her while she was being prepared for the mind-touch.

At a party on Waterdeep that same night, she related the events to a merchant, Mrilla Malsander, and then headed to Skullport. She was tracked by two of the Seven Sisters, Laeral Silverhand and Qilué Veladorn. Brelma led the two Chosen into an ambush, and then disappeared into the home of Auvrurn Labraster. They lost her track shortly after.

## Briion Dargrant

Briion Dargrant was one of the apprentices at Blackstaff Tower in the .

Personality
Some of Laeral's smiles gave him disturbed dreams. He looked forward to them, actually.

Activities
As duty apprentice, some nights Briion watched over the scrying orbs to keep watch over the Tower. More usually, he made a study of how venoms could be used as material components for spells.

Relationships
He was an apprentice at Blackstaff Tower.

History
In, one evening in which Briion was duty apprentice, Laeral Silverhand returned to the tower and asked him to burn the clothes she wore. While handling her discarded gaudy, torn robes, he noticed the smell of feline fur and viper poison, prompting him to wonder out loud where Laeral had been; she claimed she'd been kissing serpents, and asked him to tell no one about her escapade.

Later, he noticed the scrying orb watching over her casting room going dark. He deemed it to be her not wanting to be observed; however, as he turned around, the orb exploded, throwing him off his chair. He fainted.

## Branson Blackboot

Branson Blackboot was the innkeeper of the Old Man's Face inn in the village of Tyrluk in the kingdom of Cormyr in the late 15 century DR.

History
In the mid-1480s DR, Branson was involved in shady dealings—working with well-known criminals to extort money from his fellow townspeople in Tyrluk. However, after the Cormyrean adventurer-noble Oriphaun Huntsilver hid a fragment of the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll at the Old Man's Face, an army of sellswords led by the Netherese wizards Wyle and Tarys came down the High Road and broke into the inn in search of it. While the bartender, William Windsphere, hid himself in a crate in the tavern basement, Branson locked himself in his office, where he stayed until Harpers agents who were also searching for the scroll fragment arrived. When the mercenaries were killed, Branson gave the adventurers a key to the barn, where he kept the tavern key. If they had pried into his business they might have found evidence of his criminal dealings, potentially leading to a deadly confrontation or at least a bribe.

## Bregubrod Dryctcyni

Bregubrod Dryctcyni was an adventuring conjurer who found love in an ogre's embrace, marrying Willac Tatthryd, a female ogre and a fellow adventurer in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
At the age of twenty-two, Bregubrod was quite tall and gaunt:, weighing . He had long, straight black hair and brown eyes and was overall a dark and daunting person, dressed in black.

Personality
Bregubrod's personality changed after spending time with Willac Tatthryd. However, he was still a greedy man, insisting on helping others but always finding a way to help himself in the process. When an opportunity presented itself, Bregubrod eagerly exchanged spells with other mages but only if he received more than he provided in the exchange. He was emotionally distant and did not tolerate small talk, preferring magical or historical research instead.

When adventuring, Bregubrod was cautious and prepared to protect himself with prepared defensive spells or showering the undead with holy water.

Abilities
As a mage, Bregubrod was trained in fighting with a dagger. He was knowledgeable on the topics of ancient history, spellcraft, herbalism, and using a rope. He was also a skilled brewer. Bregubrod's spellbook was mostly filled with combat-oriented conjuration magics, such as shocking grasp, glitterdust, Melf's acid arrow, summon swarm, and vampiric touch. His defensive arsenal included such spells as mage armor, hypnotism, mount, mirror image, rope trick, and invisibility, among others.

Possessions
Bregubrod's leather backpack was filled with a dozen daggers, nine vials of holy water, a potion of invisibility, and a potion of mammal control.

Relationships
Bregubrod was fully devoted to his ogre wife and learned to let heckling and offensive comments go when amidst other people. Despite being a selfish man, he always sought to pamper his wife, coming up with gifts and showering her with attention.

History
Bregubrod Dryctcyni's adventuring career was quite fresh by the time he married, as he had only begun adventuring at the age of twenty-one. But his aspirations were grim: he sought to increase his magely powers and eventually become a lich. One day, he was hired as a henchman to an adventuring ogress but was horrified when it was revealed that the ogress was a morally upright human male cleric who has been reincarnated as a female ogre. Despite that, the two came to enjoy each other's company. The ogress named Willac Tatthryd often proselytized and preached good morals, and with time, it rubbed off, making Bregubrod a better man.

Bregubrod's attitude towards Willac changed to infatuation after the mage tasted an unknown potion that turned out to be a philter of love at the age of twenty-two. The wizard courted the ogress for several months until proclaiming his undying love. Willac was not used to a man's affections and this much attention. But despite it all, she developed feelings for the little man, and the two tied the knot.

## Bread Riots

Bread Riots was a violent disorder that took place in the city of Baldur's Gate in the .

History
Bread Riots erupted in the mid-13 century DR and threatened Baldur's Gate with anarchy. The rioting commoners were faced with the gauntleted hand of law, the Flaming Fist mercenary company. Suldrian, who was one of the city's ruling Dukes at the time, rode to confront rioters atop a massive, fearsome, and sepensive stallion, weaving a set of enchanted plate mail. This came to be known as Suldrian's Charge. It began with a momentary success as the Duke's glorious visage scared the rioters away. The tide quickly turned with someone launched a missile from the crowd at the armored Duke (the missile's nature was debated by historians and also became a common tavern joke and a topic of many raunchy songs). One thing was known about the missile – it was something soiled. The magic armor's only directive activated, and, to the Duke's protestations, the animated armor began polishing itself with the closest banner it could grab. Fighting against the armor's movements, Suldrian fell off his horse, and his erratic behavior scared the Flaming Fists away, thinking the leader was affected by some foul magic. Duke Suldrian was left alone with a rioting mob, and rioting mobs were not known for their mercy.

When the riots ended, Duke Suldrian's body was found, still encased in a spotlessly shone armor that was untouched by the mob. Historians speculated that the mob was scared off by the fact that, in their eyes, the Duke refused to die even after countless deadly wounds, continuing to move and shine his armor.

## Brerest

Brerest was one of the bandits under Helm Stoneblade in the early 3 century DR.

Abilities
On foot, he was among the fastest in his group.

Relationships
He was a Knight of Athalantar under Helm Stoneblade, and later a bandit.

After a successful ambush by the bandits, slaying a dozen of the armsmen under the Magelords of Athalantar, a patrol of armsmen found the way to the bandit hideout. Brerest and fellow bandit Eladar were dispatched to run out and slay any runners back that might report the location to the main group, but they were pinned down by crossbowmen. ## Broarn

Broarn was the innkeeper of the Woodsedge Inn in the early 3 century DR.

Description
He was stout-bodied, with a curling moustache.

Personality
Broarn casually spoke at length, even after killing someone.

Abilities
He could swing an axe in a deadly manner.

Possessions
He was the owner of Woodsedge Inn.

Activities
Broarn was an innkeeper.

Relationships
Helm Stoneblade spoke of him as a "quiet friend to outlaws".

History
In Hammer of the, Elminster headed for the Woodsedge Inn after Lawless Castle was attacked by the forces of the Magelords of Athalantar, trying to shake off their pursuit. On reaching the inn, he snuck into the stable; there, he was confronted by a mageling named Caladar Thearyn. The mageling paralyzed him, but was hewed down by Broarn, who struck him from behind with an axe. The innkepeer warned him that they had armsmen staying over, but brought him food, and told him extensively of the surroundings of Athalantar. He then advised him to become a thief to learn more of the magelords.

During the evening, he also allowed Helm Stoneblade and a number of his outlaws to use the stable to sleep.

## Brimstone, the Heart of the Dracolich

Brimstone, the Heart of the Dracolich was a powerful magical orb made from the heart of Karliath found in Myth Drannor in the late 14 century DR.

Powers
The Cult of the Dragon created Brimstone from the heart of the dracolich Karliath during its ritual of undeath. It was steeped in the powers of destruction, entropy, and annihilation, greatly improving spells that dealt with acid, electricity, sound waves, and cold, with a special focus on empowering fire, negative energy, and poison; it also sheltered the bearer from any destructive effects that might befall them. The necromantic spells of the bearer were given the boon of crushing despair, ineffably sapping the will to live from anyone affected by them.

History
The orb was made from the very heart of Karliath, and transformed into a powerful implement. It was later recovered from the Cult of the Dragon base hidden in Myth Drannor after the Cormanthor Elves attacked the Red Wizards of Kathul Sep and Droic Orgul there.

## Broodhull Clan

The Broodhull Clan was a mysterious dwarven tribe the once ruled over the mountains of the Vilhon Reach sometime before the 14 century DR.

History
The Broodhull Clan ruled over the mountains of the Vilhon Reach region in Central Faerûn for centuries until its mysterious disappearance. Within a single year, all two hundred members of Clan Broodhull were gone, leaving behind only empty halls. Some claimed that the clan's halls were haunted and one could still hear ghosts of the past inhabitants echoing across the empty walls. One artifact that the Broodhull Clan left behind was a glittering, rune-carved, mighty war hammer – simply called Rune Hammer.

The Brave Blades were an adventuring band in the early 3 century DR. In the, their wizard Lhangaern, accidentally killed himself with a potion he brewed; he was replaced by a priestess, Elmara (secretly Prince Elminster Aumar of Athalantar). On the, they fought and slew the Bright Shields band of adventurers in Baerlith, several of their numbers dying in the confrontation.

## Breiyr

Lord Breiyr was a frontier noble of the Sword Coast North in the 3 century DR. He was the Lord of Morlin Castle.

Description
He was stout and aging, with a ruddy face. His hands were knobbed and scarred from numerous breakings in his youth.

Personality
The man was appreciative of good food and had a rough, direct manner, but he was not above attempting to be diplomatic.

Abilities
In his youth he'd been a mighty warrior.

Possessions
Of note, he owned Morlin Castle.

Relationships
As of, he was Lord of Morlin, Lord Protector of the Sword Hills, Baron of Steeping Falls. He was widowed; his wife was "smooth-tongued", and had a daughter. This daughter was the steward of Morlin Castle.

History
When he was younger, the Lord of Morlin had fought many foes, such as warbands of orcs, hobgoblins, and even giants. He had since married, and widowed. He had also made his daughter the steward of Morlin.

On Eleint 11 of the, Breiyr received visits from both Arthlach, Axelord of Westdelve, and Falaeve, Highlord of Siluvanede; the two took a quick dislike to each other, which threatened to spill over during dinner. During their evening feast, Axelord brought up the rumors that the fires last spring had been set by sorcery, which the elven lord rejected. Then the Herald Huntinghorn arrived at the castle, and Breiyr swiftly invited him to the dinner.

The herald confirmed the rumors as far as he knew, and brought up the Magelords of Athalantar as the certain culprits. As the elven lord attempted to cast a spell, the dwarven lord responded swiftly and slew him. The elf melted into the form of a man, recognized by Breiyr's Knight of the Gate as Ubriien Orlyn, Mage Royal of Athalantar. The herald revealed himself to be Myrjala Talithyn, a local sorceress, and took her leave. Lord Breiyr then agreed with his daughter that Athalantar was probably going to be a difficult neighbor.

Reputation
There were songs about his valor.

A svirfneblin expedition, among which numbered Brickers, Belwar Dissengulp, and Drizzt Do'Urden, encountered Bruck's tribe. In the hopes of avoiding conflict, they conducted a ruse in which Drizzt claimed drow dominion over the surroundings and the svirfneblin as slaves, and successfully intimidated Bruck to leave. Some time later, the tribe was accosted by the reanimated Zaknafein Do'Urden, who, in his bloodlust, slayed Bruck and some fifteen other goblins.

When the Wailing Death struck the city of Neverwinter in the, Bruno resided and trained in the Neverwinter Academy, honing his knavish skills. ## Bronzewood tlahuitolli

Bronzewood tlahuitolli and their more powerful variant, exalted bronzewood tlahuitolli, were a variety of artifact created to be used by ranger adventurers in the Chultan city of Port Nyanzaru in the late 15 century DR.

Description
These bronzewood tlahuitolli were testaments of their owners' strength and the influence of their guilds. These magic items could be created by the most competent weaponscrafters of Chult with the use of obsidian, living bronzewood, ornamentation of jungle feathers, Chultan silk, and lakh varnish.

Powers
Bronzewood tlahuitolli could be enchanted to house various magics customized for each owner, in addition to boosting the owner's accuracy, battle prowess, and their ability to land well-aimed, devastating, and lucky strikes. When a feathered ilhuilli was wielded with a bronzewood tlahuitolli, both items boosted each other's damage output, improved healing magics, and absorbed a small portion of incoming damage.

History
Bronzewood tlahuitolli were created in the late 15 century DR, and their design suggested the items' origins to be tied to the ancient Lost City of Omu.
